APM PMS Q&A SOP: the session run on Monday 9-Nov, on the 9-Nov close

Readings as at Mon 9-Nov-2026, Monday's close (the dashboard data built at 22:47 UK). The previous note read Fri 6-Nov; this one adds one session, Monday 9-Nov, in which the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.3% and every industry rose. The Friday 6-Nov readings of Industry RS below 30 and the relative 200D MA flat or falling over the month stand until the next Friday reading, Fri 13-Nov. The next month-end reading is Mon 30-Nov. The next Tuesday session is tomorrow, 10-Nov. The rules are your Position Entry and Exit Rules as changed on 2-Nov-2026.

The three things that matter most this week

1. The rules sell about 19.8% of capital at tomorrow's session, Tue 10-Nov, all of it in Industrials and capital goods: Volex (5.61%), Sdiptech (7.36%) and Arteche (3.64%) in full, and Alfen's two tranches (3.18%).

  • Why it matters: Monday did not change any of these signals; the reason for each sale is listed under "The Tuesday 10-Nov sales the rules require" below. Volex and Sdiptech sell on the industry's Industry RS of 26 at the 6-Nov reading, not on the stocks' own readings.
  • What Monday added: Volex rose 3.1% with its peers on light volume (0.86x) and stays 3.9% below its rising absolute 200D MA; its interim results are on Thu 12-Nov, two sessions after the session the rule sells at. Sdiptech fell 0.1% while its industry rose 1.5%, the first day its own Stock RS (88 to 83) joined the weakness. Arteche fell 10% intraday to €24.4, its March low, and closed at the top of the day's range on 2.29x volume, though still down 0.7% on the day (a distribution day on your definition): a possible first sign of a low.
  • For your ruling: Sdiptech's size is in doubt (the register says 7.36%, UBS 6.04% on 5-Nov, your 4-Nov note about 6.0%).

2. IMCD's tranche 1 began a test under the 10-day stop-loss rule on Monday: day 1 of 10, a close of €102.25 against its 20D MA of €102.42 (stop-loss €96.28).

  • Why it matters: a second close at or below the 20D MA tomorrow on volume of about 100,000 shares (0.75 times the 50D average volume) confirms the exit, and the tranche (2.11%) would then sell at Tue 17-Nov. A close above about €102.38 ends the test. IMCD has four distribution days since its 14-Oct closing high (15-Oct, 5, 6 and 9-Nov), three of them in the last four sessions; estimates did not move on the 5-Nov results. I grade the exit Probable. Tranche 2 (bought 4-Nov, 50D MA) is clear.

3. Hexatronic and Avanza both face a sell signal at a reading that is days away, and both bounced on Monday.

  • Hexatronic (4.58%): relative price is 27.4% below its 52W high; if the market is unchanged from Monday, it gives a whole-holding sell signal at 30-Nov unless the SP closes at 38.60 or higher that day, 3.2% above Monday's 37.39. Monday's +2.7% on 1.49x volume bounced off 5% below the rising absolute 200D MA and closed 0.1% under it. Estimates, Buys and +100% Net Upgrade Breadth are unchanged: a de-rating, not a fundamental change. I grade the month-end sell signal Probable.
  • Avanza (5.28%): already awaiting replacement under the one-off in/out link; the relative 200D MA test reads at Fri 13-Nov and gives a third sell signal unless relative price rises about 0.7% above Monday's level from Tue 10-Nov and holds there through Friday (about 1.2% by Friday if it climbs steadily). The SP rose 7.8% in the week and regained its 150D MA on light volume (0.64x).

How this note is built

Group 1 (Market) sets the week, the fortnight and the month. Group 2 (Individual Positions) takes the 16 holdings in the order they most need your thinking: the four sales due tomorrow first, then IMCD's test. Group 3 (Portfolio) gives the themes across holdings, each holding's add stage, the graded add and exit candidates and the proposed order of sale under the link; then what I will do unless you say otherwise, and the data faults.

Group 1 (Market)

The Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.3% on Monday 9-Nov, which leaves it up 1.1% in the week, 2.5% over the fortnight and 4.0% over the month; over three months it is down 0.4%, over twelve months up 16.2%. The bases: the week is the 5 sessions from the 2-Nov close, the fortnight the 10 sessions from the 26-Oct close and the month the 21 sessions from the 9-Oct close, the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's bases; the industry figures are price moves of each industry's index over the same sessions (the Market chart, 3 and 12 months).

  • Monday 9-Nov: every industry rose. Defence led (+3.9%), then Industrials and capital goods and Energy, commodities and metals mining (+1.5% each) and Financials (+1.3%); Media (+0.1%), Transportation and Professional, business and consumer services (+0.2% each) and Consumer staples (+0.3%) rose least.
  • The week: Media +3.1%, Financials +2.5%, Healthcare and Utilities +2.4%, Real assets +2.3% and Telecoms +2.2% led; Transportation (3.2%), Technology (3.0%), Defence (1.5%), Energy, commodities and metals mining (1.0%) and Industrials and capital goods (0.3%) fell.
  • The month's leaders (21 sessions to 9-Nov): Healthcare +9.4%, Consumer staples +8.5%, Consumer discretionary +7.4% and Real assets +7.3%. The laggards: Technology (4.2%), Transportation (3.3%), Energy, commodities and metals mining (3.1%) and Industrials and capital goods (0.5%).
  • Over three months: Healthcare +8.9%, Consumer staples +7.0% and Transportation +4.3% lead; Defence (10.3%), Real assets (4.9%), Technology (2.6%), Media (2.6%), Materials (2.0%) and Industrials and capital goods (1.7%) lag.

Monday was a broad risk-on day, and it carried the book's weakest holdings with it rather than on their own. Theon rose 4.9%, 1.26 times Defence's +3.9%, on ordinary volume; Volex rose 3.1% with its peers (+3.3% on average) on light volume; Hexatronic rose 2.7% on 1.49x volume. Against that, five holdings fell in a market up 1.3%: Carlsberg 0.8%, Arteche 0.7%, IMCD 0.5% (the day its tranche 1's test began), CVS Group 0.1% and Sdiptech 0.1%; Fevertree gapped up 2.2% and sold back most of the gain to close up 0.4%. What would confirm a turn in the weak holdings rather than a one-day lift: a second up day on volume of 1.5 times the 50D average volume or more; a fall back on heavier volume than Monday's would say the lift was the market's alone.

The defensive rotation is still the month's story, but it paused this week. Healthcare (+9.4%) and Consumer staples (+8.5%) lead the month, yet Consumer staples rose only 1.5% in the week, just above the index's 1.1%, and 0.3% on Monday, below the index's 1.3%. Carlsberg (+1.2% in the week), Hilton Food (+0.7%) and Fevertree ((1.8%)) all lagged Consumer staples' week. What would confirm the rotation resuming: Consumer staples leading again into the 13-Nov Friday reading; a second week of Financials and Media leading would say money is moving back to cyclicals.

The AI and data-centre complex is the book's weak spot over the month: Technology (4.2%) is the worst industry and Industrials and capital goods (0.5%) is fourth worst. The holdings in them fell much further over the month: Arteche (22.5%), Volex (22.1%), Hexatronic (11.1%), Alfen (8.7%). Over the week Hexatronic fell 15.6%, Arteche 7.2% and Volex 5.5% against Industrials and capital goods' (0.3%), so most of the week's fall was the stocks' own; the sources read name no company news for Hexatronic or Arteche.

Industrials and capital goods, the book's largest industry (about 23.5% of capital on current values: Sdiptech 7.36%, Volex 5.61%, IMCD 3.69%, Arteche 3.64%, Alfen 3.18%), read Industry RS 26 at the 6-Nov reading, from 46 at 30-Oct. The industry's index is down only 0.5% over the month: its Industry RS fell because other industries rose, not because it collapsed. The reading stands until Fri 13-Nov and sells Volex and Sdiptech in full tomorrow; with Arteche and Alfen also sold tomorrow, the only holding left in the industry would be IMCD, at 3.69% of capital.

Defence fell 10.3% over three months, and Theon's Industry RS is 13. Monday's 3.9% rise leaves the industry down 1.5% over the week and up 6.2% over the month. The Portfolio Fitness for Fighting table holds Defence at Underweight.

The Portfolio Fitness for Fighting assessment of the ideal portfolio (the table set on 6-Nov-2026, unchanged): Overweight Consumer staples and Energy, commodities and metals mining; Double Underweight Consumer discretionary and Real assets; Underweight Defence, Media and Telecoms; Neutral elsewhere, Industrials and capital goods included; Momentum Underweight and Fragility Double Underweight. Its logic, in brief: risk-off beneath an index near its high on narrow breadth, defensives leading: own the defensives with rising estimates, avoid the leveraged and rate-sensitive. This week's tape went against the table: Underweight Media (+3.1%) led the week and Double Underweight Real assets (+2.3%) rose with it, while Overweight Energy, commodities and metals mining fell 1.0% and Overweight Consumer staples (+1.5%) only just beat the index (+1.1%). After tomorrow's sales the book's Consumer staples weight (Carlsberg 4.59%, Hilton Food 4.77%, Fevertree 2.47%, about 11.8%) would be about three times its Industrials and capital goods weight.

Among the holdings, relative price is more than 25% below its 52W high for Alfen (49.9%), Arteche (43.6%), Xvivo (39.2%), Theon (28.2%), Hexatronic (27.4%) and Volex (26.5%); the test reads only at the month-end, and at 30-Oct only Alfen, Arteche and Xvivo were past it (Theon read 20.8%).

Middle Innings Core Investment · 5.61% of capital in 3 tranches (tranche 1, 2.99%, bought 10-Jul-2026; tranche 2, 1.61%, bought 5-Aug-2026; tranche 3, 1.01%, bought 29-Oct-2026) · readings as at Mon 9-Nov-2026

Volex bounced 3.1% to 536p on Monday 9-Nov, in step with its seven closest peers and on light volume; the break below the rising absolute 200D MA stands, 3.9% below it.

  • Volex is 23.3% below its 699p closing high of 6-Oct: (5.5%) over the week, (22.1%) over the month, (5.3%) over 3 months, +45.3% over 12 months.
  • Volex's legs since 6-Oct: 699p to 532p (22-Oct), (23.9%); 532p to 590p (29-Oct), +10.9%; 590p to 520p (6-Nov), (11.9%), five down days in six; 520p to 536p (9-Nov), +3.1%, one session.
  • Over 5 sessions Volex's own move, after the part its beta of 1.49 to the Stoxx Europe 600 explains, is (7.1%), 1.2 times its usual weekly move; over 20 sessions it is (26.5%), 2.1 times.

Leg by leg, the second leg down is the basket's with Volex-specific selling on top; Monday is the basket's alone.

  • 29-Oct to 6-Nov, (11.9%): the cohort peers fell with Volex on 30-Oct ((2.1%)), 4-Nov ((3.3%), while the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.8% on defensives) and 5-Nov (Technology (2.5%); Oxford Instruments (6.7%), TT Electronics (3.8%)); on 6-Nov five of the seven peers rose while Volex fell 0.8%.
  • 9-Nov +3.1%: Industrials +1.5%, Technology +1.0%, Stoxx Europe 600 +1.3%; peers +3.3% on average (item 9 (Peers)). Volex matched them.
  • Hypotheses, ranked: (1) a rotation out of AI data-centre and technology hardware into defensives, now pausing: the peers fell with Volex on 30-Oct, 4 and 5-Nov and rose with it on 9-Nov. (2) Holders cutting Volex ahead of the 12-Nov results: Volex has fallen about 4.8 points more than its peers since 29-Oct. Test: Volex's 12-Nov move against the basket's.

Developments: Richard's weekly AlphaSense report was not read this run; the newest read is that of 6-Nov-2026, which has "Volex PLC — No Material Weekly Developments". Standing: 19-Oct, the trading update (Sector Digest insight, 3-Nov), profit guided "towards the upper end" but data-centre revenue down on the prior half and about $10m pulled forward (Volex (11.6%)); 30-Oct, H1 FY2027 interim results set for Thursday 12-Nov-2026 (IRPRESS, via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 4-Nov-2026). The causes of the 5, 6 and 9-Nov moves are not in the sources read; a web search on 9-Nov found no Volex news.

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "[-] Volex bouncing between the rising 200 and 150D after a large 20% fall through Oct-26. Must get comfortable with SS momentum etc." The bounce between those MAs ended on 4-Nov: Volex is 3.9% below the rising absolute 200D MA (557.68p) and 9.2% below the rising 150D MA (590.09p). The retest you asked the APM to confirm on 28-Oct did not hold.

The MAs: Volex is below every MA, the 5D (539.00p) included, and the short MAs keep falling through the long ones. The 20D MA (574.10p) crossed below the 150D on 6-Nov and below the 100D (577.54p) on 9-Nov, and is now 0.6% under it; the 10D (555.40p) is below the absolute 200D. The 100D is falling over 5 and 20 sessions; the 50D (610.58p) has fallen 1.0% over 5 sessions but is still up 0.8% over 20; the 150D and absolute 200D still rise, and the absolute 200D keeps rising over the next two weeks because the closes it drops are 437p to 476p. Precedent: every low of the advance since mid-2025 held above the rising absolute 200D MA; the closes below it since 22-Oct are Volex's first since 5-Jun-2025.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Volex against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes (+2.6% over 20 sessions, slowing). The 150D above the absolute 200D: passes. The 50D above the 150D and absolute 200D: passes, but the 50D has fallen 1.0% in 5 sessions.
  • The SP above each MA: fails; 3.9% below the absolute 200D, 7.2% below the 100D, 9.2% below the 150D, 12.2% below the 50D. Near its 52W high: fails; 23.3% below. Away from its 52W low: passes; 43.9% above 372.46p.
  • Stage 3 flags present: the largest one-day fall since the advance began, after good news (19-Oct, (11.6%)); wider swings ((25.6%) against 6% to 7% pullbacks in August and September); lower lows (520p on 6-Nov below 22-Oct's 532p); more down days than up days (14 against 10 since 6-Oct); the SP tracking the rising absolute 200D MA from 30-Oct to 3-Nov, then breaking it. Absent: a light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume (the 6-Oct high was an accumulation day).
  • Verdict: a broken trend on the medium-term tests, confirmed by the 5-Nov volume; the long-term structure (rising 150D and absolute 200D MAs) is not yet broken, and Monday's light-volume bounce does not change that. Four of six trend-health tests pass, two fail; five of six Stage 3 flags show. What would settle it: a heavy-volume close back above 557.7p would reopen a retest; a close below 497p (the 8-Jul low) breaks the July base.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Basis: Volex's volume ratios leave out the mechanical-volume day of 18-Sep, the only one in the 50-session window (31-Aug was not a trading day), which lifts the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's 50D average by about 15%; the dashboard's figure follows in brackets.

Monday's 3.1% rise came on light volume: no buyer has yet shown size at the low. On 9-Nov Volex traded 0.78M shares (£4.2m), 0.86x (0.75x) its 50D average, opened unchanged at 520p, dipped to 517.3p and closed at 536p, 0.79 of the day's range. The cause of Volex's own rise is not in the sources read; its seven closest peers rose with it (item 9 (Peers)).

Volex's heavy-volume days of the last 25 sessions (1.5x the 50D average or more): seven, five of them down or flat, and the two largest bracket the decline.

  • Up: 6-Oct, the 699p high, +3.2%, 1.36M shares (£9.5m), 1.64x (1.41x); 16-Oct, +0.5%, 1.39M (£8.9m), 1.73x (1.48x).
  • Down or flat: 8-Oct (1.0%), 1.80M (£12.2m), 2.23x (1.91x); 15-Oct (5.9%), 1.85M (£11.7m), 2.34x (2.00x), a 1.3% gap down in a US-led technology sell-off (Hargreaves Lansdown, 15-Oct); 19-Oct (11.6%), 3.28M (£18.4m), 3.80x (3.29x), closing at the day's low on the trading update; 20-Oct (0.2%), 2.12M (£11.9m), 2.37x (2.07x).
  • 5-Nov (4.6%), 3.50M shares (£18.4m), 3.83x (3.35x) on today's 50D average, a 1.1% gap down, an intraday low of 515p, close at 0.20 of the range; no Volex news in the sources read. That 515p low held on 6 and 9-Nov (lows 515p and 517.3p).

Distribution days in 25 sessions (dashboard): 5 (8, 15, 19 and 20-Oct, and 5-Nov), up from 4 at 26-Oct and 2 at 12-Oct. 8-Oct leaves the window on Thursday 12-Nov, results day, so a results-day distribution day would keep the count at 5.

Up-day against down-day volume is weaker than in any Volex decline of the last year.

  • Over 5 sessions: one up day (0.78M shares) against four down days averaging 1.50M, a ratio of 0.52 on average volumes (dashboard 0.45). Over 20 sessions: 0.64 (dashboard 0.60).
  • Ratio of up-day to down-day volume over the decline from 6-Oct to 9-Nov: 0.68 (14 down days, 5 heavy; 10 up days, 1 heavy), against 0.76 for the May to July decline and 0.99 for the February to March decline; Volex's advances ran at 1.13 to 1.51.

Volume at the MAs: every break came on more volume than the reclaim before it. The 50D, 100D and 150D MAs broke on 19-Oct on 3.80x (3.29x); the 100D and 150D were reclaimed on 29-Oct on 0.50x (0.43x), and the 50D has not been reclaimed since. The absolute 200D MA was reclaimed on 27-Oct on 0.86x (0.76x), lost on 4-Nov on 0.90x (0.78x), and the SP fell further below it on 5-Nov on 3.83x (3.35x).

Volume has not dried up through the move: Volex's 20D average volume is 1.32 times its 50D average without the mechanical-volume days (1.15 times with them; the dashboard's trend reading is 1.02, on an unstated basis). A healthy pullback dries up into its low; this one has not.

Precedent: Volex's last two declines ended with heavy-volume up days within six sessions of the low (after 8-Jul: 2.0x, 2.3x and 1.7x; after 17-Mar: 1.7x and 3.7x). Since 22-Oct the best Volex up day reached 1.28x (1.12x).

Relative price confirmed the SP's lower low, and never confirmed the 6-Oct high. Volex's relative price is 26.5% below its 28-May 52W high on 9-Nov (28.0% on 5 and 6-Nov, 21.6% at the 30-Oct reading) and 9.7% below its relative 200D MA, lost on 30-Oct. At the 6-Oct SP high it stood 1.1% short of its own high. The dashboard's trigger price for the 25% line is 547.15p at a flat market, 2.1% above the close.

Peers' volume: DiscoverIE and Halma sold on about 3x with Volex on 5-Nov; on 9-Nov DiscoverIE rose 3.8% on 2.31x while Volex rose 3.1% on 0.86x: the basket's buyer came back to DiscoverIE, not to Volex.

What volume would confirm: a turn: an up day on about 1.4M shares or more (1.5x without the mechanical-volume days) closing in the top third of its range and above 557.7p (the absolute 200D MA), most plausibly on the 12-Nov results. The move continues: a close below 515p, then 497p (the 8-Jul low), on above-average volume, or a distribution day on results day.

3. RS

Volex's Stock RS and Sector RS rose five points each on Monday and now sit on their lines; Industry RS, at 26, has not moved. Readings of 9-Nov: Stock RS 51, Sector RS 50, Industry RS 26 (6-Nov: 46, 45, 26).

  • Volex's Stock RS: 97 on 9-Oct, 65 on 23-Oct, 85 on 29-Oct, 78 at the 30-Oct reading, 60 on 4-Nov, 46 on 6-Nov, 51 on 9-Nov: still 46 points down in a month, below the 70 line and 1 point above the 50 line.
  • Volex's Sector RS (Engineered components, polymers and electronics manufacturing): 74 on 9-Oct, 70 at 30-Oct, 45 on 6-Nov, 50 on 9-Nov: on the line, since the test reads "below 50".
  • Volex's Industry RS (Industrials and capital goods): 79 on 9-Oct, 46 at 30-Oct, 26 on 4, 6 and 9-Nov. The 6-Nov Friday reading of 26 is the sell signal and stands until the Friday 13-Nov reading. Industry RS moves in steps of about 6.7 points, one place among 16 industries, so 30 needs one place.

The order of the turn: the industry first, then Volex, then its sector: a rotation carrying the stock. Volex's Industry RS fell first (79 to 66 by 16-Oct, while Volex's Stock RS was still 94); Volex's Stock RS fell 29 points in its update week (65 on 23-Oct); the sector held 69 to 79 until 30-Oct and fell 25 points in the next week. Over the last 20 sessions the Market chart shows Healthcare +8.9% and Consumer staples +7.7% against the Stoxx Europe 600's +3.3%, Industrials and capital goods' (0.7%) and Technology's (4.4%). On 9-Nov Industrials rose 1.5%: one day, not yet a turn.

Volex's peers' Stock RS fell with it, which supports the rotation reading: on 9-Nov Huber + Suhner read 8 (44 a month ago), DiscoverIE 73 (94), AQ Group 57 (77); TT Electronics, at 93, and Solid State, at 40 (25), are the exceptions.

Precedent: when Volex's Industry RS last stood near the line, at 40 in March 2026, Volex's Stock RS held 84 to 86 and Volex rose 63% from its 17-Mar low; this time the stock broke with the industry. Test: Volex's Stock RS after the 12-Nov results; back above 70 would say the stock leads again.

4. Consensus by named FY

Volex's consensus has not moved since the 6-Nov run while the SP fell 23.3% from its 6-Oct high; the estimates still rise at every horizon over 1, 3 and 6 months, at a one-month pace slowed to 2% to 3%.

  • Volex's FY2027 (year to March 2027) EPS, $0.521 (8-Nov): +2.9% over 1 month, +15.0% over 3 months, +20.0% over 6 months. FY2028 EPS, $0.579: +2.5%, +14.4%, +21.1%. FY2029 EPS, $0.638: +2.2%, +13.1% over 3 months (first estimate 30-Apr).
  • Volex's FY2027 revenue, $1,411m: +1.8%, +6.8%, +12.2%; FY2027 EBITDA, $192.6m: +2.0%, +10.5%, +21.4%; FY2028 and FY2029 at about the same pace (7 analysts each).
  • Trajectory: the later years rise a little more slowly than FY2027 over 3 months, so implied FY2027 to FY2028 EPS growth eased from 11.7% (31-Jul) to 11.1%, and FY2028 to FY2029 from 11.6% to 10.3%: analysts are raising the level faster than the trajectory, a mild pulling forward.
  • Margin: Volex's implied FY2027 EBITDA margin is 13.7% (13.2% at 31-Jul), FY2028's 13.8% and FY2029's 13.9%: the upgrades are margin first.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "ISSUE — Why are FY 2028 EPS estimates falling? MAJOR ISSUE." On the current series they are not: FY2028 EPS is up 2.5% in a month and 14.4% in three, and FY2028 EBITDA up 1.8% and 10.4%. You also wrote: "SS EPS breadth turned negative in Jun-Aug 26." EPS Net Upgrade Breadth never went below zero: +50% at the June month-end (4 raising, 1 cutting), +67% in July, +100% since September; the zero readings were EBITDA (April and June) and PT (June).

The SP led the estimates up to 6-Oct and has fallen since while FY2027 EPS rose about 3%: either the SP is leading the estimates down, or the market has de-rated estimates it still believes. On 3-Nov you wrote: "Almost always, price moves first". Hypothesis 1, price leads: the market is discounting a FY2028 risk that consensus does not show, the largest customer (about 18.7% of FY2026 revenue, in effect the data-centre line) in the 2026 to 2028 re-award window (Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo, 4-Nov). Hypothesis 2, de-rating: the chain is upgraded and de-rated at once (item 9 (Peers)). The tests are the memo's, all on Thursday 12-Nov: H1 underlying operating profit at or above $74m; working capital falling from 28.4% of revenue; the data-centre share of Complex Industrial Technology restated. Volex passes your 3-Nov "inviolate rule" on upgrades over three to six months; the memo expects the 3-month figure to fall to low single digits by late January on window arithmetic alone.

  • Industry RS (Industrials and capital goods): 26 on 9-Nov and at the Friday 6-Nov reading, from 46 (30-Oct), 79 (9-Oct) and 53 (7-Aug); its 13-month low before November was 40 (March 2026). It is a rank among 16 industries in steps of about 6.7: 26 is 12th place, one place below the line

The order of the turn: the industry first (after 9-Oct), the sector second (after 16-Oct), and Sdiptech held until Monday. Over the month to 9-Nov its industry fell 0.5% and Sdiptech rose 2.4%. On Monday the industry rose 1.5%, the sector's large members 2.0% to 3.2%, and Sdiptech fell 0.1%. On 3-Nov you wrote: "Otherwise, Industrials are weak sectorially".

Hypotheses, ranked: (1) money rotating within the sector towards its laggards: on Monday the groups whose Stock RS had fallen most over the month rose most and Sdiptech, the leader, lagged (item 9 (Peers)); this is the pattern you described for healthcare on 3-Nov ("It's simple reversion (short covering, or buying the laggards) - classic counter-momentum"); against it, one session is thin evidence. (2) Sdiptech's own story intact: its estimates rose again in the month (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)) and the volume was light; Stock RS of 83 is level with its August high.

Precedent: at the March 2026 low in Industry RS (40), Sdiptech's Stock RS rose from 38 to 56 inside its 45.6% advance; the rule has never fired on this holding before.

4. Consensus by named FY

Consensus has not changed since the 6-Nov reading (latest consensus 8-Nov); over the month FY2026 EPS is weaker, FY2027 and FY2028 are raised, and the margin slips.

  • FY2026 (year to Dec-26): EPS (8.0%) in a month to SEK 13.16 (from 14.30 at 30-Sep), reversing an 8.1% rise in September, so (0.5%) over 3 months and (15.5%) over 12; revenue +2.9% in a month to SEK 5,210m; EBITDA +3.1% to SEK 1,214m. EBITDA rose, so the EPS cut arises below the EBITDA line
  • FY2027: EPS +2.1% in a month to SEK 17.79 (+7.9% over 3 months, +8.5% over 6); revenue +5.2% to SEK 5,647m (+6.8% over 3 months); EBITDA +2.8% to SEK 1,416m (+4.0% over 3 months)
  • FY2028: EPS +3.7% in a month to SEK 19.97 (+9.2% over 3 months); revenue +5.9% to SEK 6,047m; EBITDA +3.4% to SEK 1,523m

The later year rises faster, so analysts are raising the trajectory, not pulling earnings forward: implied EPS growth from FY2027 to FY2028 rose from 10.6% (30-Sep) to 12.3%.

The margin keeps slipping. Sdiptech's implied FY2027 EBITDA margin (EBITDA divided by revenue) is 25.1%, from 25.7% at 30-Sep and 25.9% at 30-Apr; FY2028's is 25.2%, from 25.8% at 30-Sep. The third-quarter report explains part of it: Supply Chain & Transportation's margin fell to 18.5% from 21.0% on cost increases at Mecno, which the chief executive expects to continue through the fourth quarter; organic adjusted EBITA grew 6% against organic sales growth of 13%; and operating cash flow fell 44% to SEK 143m, a negative the consensus lines do not show (earnings review).

The FY2026 EPS cut has named parts worth about 3% of the 8%: the SEK 13m bond-redemption charge (about 2%) and DNB Carnegie's 1% trim on higher segment costs (earnings review). Hypothesis: the rest is brokers' different EPS definitions; the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (31-Oct) calls FactSet's EPS consensus "a median of two brokers on two different EPS definitions". Test: FY2027 EPS rising while FY2026 settles.

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". Sdiptech passes it: FY2027 EPS up 7.9% over 3 months and 8.5% over 6, revenue up 6.8% and 10.7%. The estimates and the SP have parted since mid-October: FY2027 EPS rose 2.1% from 30-Sep to 8-Nov while the SP closed 3.8% below its 14-Oct high; on your 3-Nov note ("Almost always, price moves first"), that is the reading to watch.

Net Upgrade Breadth is broad on revenue and EBITDA and narrow on EPS and PT, the order of item 4 (Consensus by named FY): upgrades that stop short of earnings and value. All readings are unchanged since 6-Nov.

  • Revenue (FY2027): +83% (5 raising, none cutting, of 6), from +67% at the September and October month-ends; the 100-day count that the sell test of SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50 reads stood at +71% at 30-Oct
  • EBITDA (FY2027): +83% (5 of 6 raising), from +67% in September
  • EPS (FY2027): +40% (3 raising, 1 cutting, of 5), from +80% in September and +100% in April and May
  • PT: +43% (3 raising, none cutting, of 7), from +100% in May and June
  • Buys: 5 of 6; the first non-Buy in 24 months appeared at the October month-end

Where analysts are cutting, and why. One analyst cut FY2027 EPS; the sources read name neither that analyst nor the one behind the non-Buy. DNB Carnegie (Carl Korsheden) trimmed 2026 adjusted EPS 1% on higher segment costs and his PT to SEK 340 from 342, "citing a reduction in peer group multiples and compressed earnings yield spreads in the capital goods sector" (earnings review, 23-Oct), while keeping 2027 adjusted EPS at SEK 19.5. Morgan Stanley also cut a PT while raising EPS, for Legrand on 6-Nov, "on sector de-rating" (via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026). Hypothesis: brokers are cutting industrial multiples while raising estimates, the price side of the rotation that sent Industry RS to 26; against it, RedEye raised its Sdiptech base case to SEK 315 (29-Oct, via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 4-Nov-2026).

The average PT fell 1.5% when a seventh analyst arrived, not on a cut. It moved from 312.7 (26 to 28-Oct, 6 analysts) to 308.1 (29-Oct, 7 analysts; the first non-Buy rating appeared on 30-Oct) with no PT cut counted. If the other six were unchanged, the seventh analyst's PT is about SEK 281 (7 times 308.1 less 6 times 312.7), 7.7% above Monday's close: the most cautious view on Sdiptech, and possibly the non-Buy.

Ratings carry no signal; the PT does the work. Every covering analyst rated Sdiptech a Buy through its fall to 167.0 (9-Feb-2026) and its 56% rise since; the average PT followed the SP, down 35% from August 2024 to January 2026 and up 19% since.

Hypothesis: the average PT has capped Sdiptech's SP when it stood about 12% to 16% above it, and November fits so far. Three times in 2026 (25-May, the August month-end, 14-Oct) Sdiptech came that close, then fell 6.6% to 7.8% before analysts raised the PT. On 5-Nov the PT stood 14.5% above the 269.2 intraday high and the SP turned down; on Monday the gap is 18.3%. Test: a close above 270.6 with no PT rise breaks the pattern.

Precedent: Sdiptech's SP turned with breadth from January to April 2026 (EPS breadth (100%) to +100%, SP +45.6%). Test: EPS and PT Net Upgrade Breadth at the 30-Nov month-end.

Arteche

Middle Innings Core Investment · 3.64% of capital in 3 tranches (tranche 1, 1.59%, bought 26-Jun-2026; tranche 2, 1.20%, bought 19-Aug-2026; tranche 3, 0.85%, bought 29-Oct-2026) · readings as at Mon 9-Nov-2026

1. SP and MAs

Arteche fell 10.0% intraday on 9-Nov, to €24.4, and closed at €26.9, (0.7%) on the day and at the top of the day's range, on its heaviest volume in 25 sessions; on the same day its sector bounced and Arteche missed it. Arteche is down 7.2% in the week (from €29.0 on 2-Nov), 12.1% since the 30-Oct month-end (€30.6) and 28.6% from its €37.7 close of 30-Sep. Over 3 months it is down 12.4% and over 12 months up 31.9%; the close is 38.9% below the €44.0 52W closing high (8-May-2026) and is Arteche's lowest close since 8-Apr-2026 (€26.13). Taking out the market's part (beta 0.92), Arteche's own move over the five sessions to 9-Nov is (8.2%), 1.1 times its usual weekly move of 7.7% (the standard deviation of its five-session moves over 12 months after the market's part); over 20 sessions its own move is (25.9%), 1.5 times the usual 20-session move.

The 9-Nov session, against the industry (Industrials and capital goods, Market chart) and the sector (chart files): Arteche opened 1.5% lower at €26.7, traded down to €24.4 and closed at €26.9, at 0.96 of the day's range. The Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.3%, the industry 1.5% and the cohort peers 1.5% on average; Prysmian rose 5.9%, Siemens Energy 5.1%, Legrand 3.3% and NKT 3.2%. 9-Nov was the third session in six on which Arteche fell while the large grid names rose (2, 6 and 9-Nov); on the day Arteche lagged the market's part by about 1.9 points.

The €24.4 intraday low sits on Arteche's March 2026 low: €24.35, the close and low of 30-Mar-2026, from which Arteche rose 81% to its €44.0 close of 8-May. Arteche's 10.2% recovery from the day's low to the close is its third largest in 12 months, after 7 and 8-May. The difference that matters: on 30-Mar Arteche's close sat on its rising 50D MA (€24.24) and 31.5% above its absolute 200D MA (€18.51), a retest inside an uptrend; on 9-Nov the same price is 13.3% below a rising absolute 200D MA and below all seven MAs.

Developments: Richard's weekly AlphaSense report was not read this run (the AlphaSense sign-in form showed; no Chrome browser was connected). The newest report read, of 6-Nov-2026 (covering about 30-Oct to 6-Nov), found "No material news flow", "No new sell-side notes" and "No new corporate filings or press releases" for Arteche. One web search on 9-Nov (Spanish) found nothing on Arteche dated November 2026. The cause of the 9-Nov intraday fall and recovery is not in the sources read. The dated item that bears on the window is unchanged: the Strategic Plan 2027-2029, dated 30-Nov by the company's investor agenda, the day after Spain's 29-Nov election and the month-end reading day.

Hypothesis 1 (supported by the close, on one session's evidence): 9-Nov was a forced or large seller cleared in the morning and absorbed by buyers at the March low, the first possible selling climax of the decline.

  • For it: the heaviest volume of the decline came on the day's 10% drop, and the close came back to within €0.1 of the high; the low held exactly at the March 2026 low; the volume came on a day the sector rose, so the selling was Arteche's own and someone took the other side.
  • Against it: Arteche still closed down on heavy volume, a distribution day on your definition; it missed a sector bounce of 3% to 6% at the large names; and a reversal day needs a follow-through day to count. Arteche's last two heavy-volume reversal days (a low, then a recovery on heavy volume) are mixed precedents: 2-Mar-2026 (low €22.96, close €25.14 on 2.23x) held, and the close was 6.3% higher ten sessions later; 29 and 30-Jul (2.02x and 2.14x) ended the summer decline.
  • Test: the 10-Nov session, the session the rules sell at: an up day on 1.5x the 50D average or more closing above €27.7 (the 5-Nov close) would confirm it; a close below €24.4 would refute it.

Hypothesis 2: the decline continues as small-cap holders leave a broken chart before the 30-Nov plan. Hypothesis 2 fits Cembre, the other small cohort maker, which fell 0.7% on 1.5x the same day (item 9 (Peers)), but it does not fit Arteche's own close at the day's high.

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Arteche SP chart very negative L3D. Why is that. Something is happening. Down 15% in 2D. Up 2% today. Why such a big decline". The sources read still name no company cause. Of the six sessions since 30-Oct, three (2, 6 and 9-Nov) were Arteche's own, two (4 and 5-Nov) the sector's, and one (3-Nov) a small rise.

The MAs: Arteche lost its 20D MA on 8-Oct, its 100D and 150D MAs on 19-Oct, its 50D and absolute 200D MAs on 22-Oct, regained the absolute 200D MA on 27-Oct and lost it again on 30-Oct, all on light volume (0.60x to 0.87x without mechanical-volume days). 2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Basis: ratios leave out the two mechanical-volume days in Arteche's 50D average (31-Aug and the 18-Sep index review: about 25,700 shares a day without them, 32,800 with), with the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's figure in brackets. 18-Sep leaves the 50D window on 27-Nov.

Selling volume in Arteche rose again on 9-Nov, but for the first time in the decline the heavy volume came with a close at the day's high: the most informative volume day since 30-Jul. The five sessions to 9-Nov averaged 34,100 shares a day, against 29,200 in the five to 6-Nov and 13,500 in the week to 30-Oct.

Heavy-volume days (1.5x or more) in the last 25 sessions: two, 5-Nov (1.61x (1.26x), (4.5%), closing at 0.25 of the range) and 9-Nov.

  • 9-Nov: 58,899 shares, €1.58m, 2.29x (1.72x on the dashboard's data file, which records 56,511 shares); (0.7%), a 1.5% gap down at the open, a low of €24.4 (10.0% below the 6-Nov close), and a close at €26.9, 0.96 of the day's range. The intraday low went 7.5% below €26.39, the level at which tranches 1 and 2 would be 30% below their highest close since purchase; the close is 1.9% above that level.

Distribution days on your definition (a down day on 1.25x the 50D average or more, last 25 sessions): three, all since 2-Nov (2-Nov 1.41x, 5-Nov 1.61x, 9-Nov 2.29x); on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's basis one (9-Nov). A month ago there was one (1-Oct). No accumulation day has come in 25 sessions on either basis. The dashboard counts one distribution day, and the count is right on its own basis: it sets every day against 1.25 times the latest 50D average (40,984 shares on 9-Nov), and 5-Nov's 40,032 shares fall just short of that line. The point your definition misses: 9-Nov counts as distribution because it closed 0.7% down, yet buyers took it from €24.4 to €26.9; a down close at 0.96 of the range is the opposite of the 1-Oct and 5-Nov closes (0.31 and 0.25).

Up-day volume over down-day volume (average shares): 0.58 over 5 sessions (the dashboard reads 0.0 on its five-session window, which holds no up day), 0.73 over 20 (dashboard 0.72), and 0.72 over the whole decline from 30-Sep (10 up days averaging 18,700 shares, 18 down days 26,200). In the May to July 2026 decline (without the 25-Jun placement) the ratio was 0.99: buyers are thinner in this decline than in the last one of similar size.

Volume at the MAs: the breaks of the 50D and absolute 200D MAs (22 and 30-Oct) came on light volume, 0.79x and 0.60x; the moves away from them came on 1.41x (2-Nov), 1.61x (5-Nov) and 2.29x (9-Nov). Precedent: how Arteche's 2026 declines ended. The June to July decline ended with a 6.1% up day on 2.14x on 30-Jul, the session after the low, closing at its high. The March 2026 decline ended at €24.35 on 0.37x volume, a quiet low. 9-Nov is closest to 29 and 30-Jul compressed into one session: heavy selling to a low and a close at the high; what it lacks is the up close.

It closed 0.1% below its 5D MA (€10.32), 0.2% below its falling 20D (€10.33, down 2.6% in five sessions), 7.6% below its falling 50D (€11.16), 13.8% below its absolute 200D (€11.96, down 0.4% over 20 sessions), 15.9% below its falling 100D (€12.26) and 19.4% below its 150D (€12.79), from which the tranches' stop-loss is set. The 50D MA has been below the absolute 200D MA since 14-Oct; after the March low it took eight weeks to rise back above it. The 20D MA, crossed on heavy volume on 4 and 5-Nov, has not been regained on a close since.

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: fails ((0.42%) over 20 sessions).
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: passes (€12.79 against €11.96).

Three intraday tests of about €9.92 (27-Oct, 5 and 6-Nov) have held on a closing basis. Hypothesis: sellers are thinning near €9.9. Against it: no rebound from there has come on heavy volume, and 9-Nov's light lag says buyers are not chasing Alfen either.

The 26-Oct low (€9.95) resembles the quiet 29-Jul low, whose rally failed: ten sessions on, the only heavy up day (4-Nov) closed in the bottom half of its range.

On 31-Oct-2025 its Stock RS was 3 with Sector RS 86, and the SP rose 19.3% from its 3-Nov-2025 low to 4-Feb-2026; on 20-Mar-2026, 12 with Sector RS 74, and the SP rose 138% to 26-May. 4. Consensus by named FY

Alfen's EBITDA consensus is falling for every year and its revenue consensus is edging up: more revenue at a lower margin, the pattern since the half-year results of 18-Aug. Nothing changed in the latest reading (8-Nov) against the previous run. (Years to December; EUR.)

  • EBITDA: FY2026 €21.65m, (0.4%) over 1 month, (14.4%) over 3 and (12.3%) over 6; FY2027 €27.95m, (2.9%), (9.0%) and (3.2%); FY2028 €33.71m, (2.2%), (6.5%) and (2.2%). The latest cuts came on 4 and 6-Nov, after the results.
  • Revenue: FY2026 €458.1m, FY2027 €495.5m, FY2028 €537.2m, each up 0.6% over 1 month and 1.3% to 1.8% over 3.
  • EPS: FY2026 a loss of €(0.140), against a €0.18 profit three months ago; FY2027 €0.350, (1.3%) over 1 month, +11.8% over 3; FY2028 €0.460, (7.8%) and (4.6%). On six or seven analysts and a near-zero base, EBITDA is the cleaner line.

The later years are cut as well as the nearer one, so analysts are lowering the trajectory, not pulling earnings forward. Implied EBITDA margins are 4.7% (FY2026), 5.6% (FY2027) and 6.3% (FY2028). Implied EBITDA growth of 29.1% in FY2027 and 20.6% in FY2028, on revenue growth of 8.2% and 8.4%, places the recovery in the margin, and each round of cuts moves it out.

The narrowed guidance of 4-Nov points to a weak fourth quarter. On the APM's arithmetic, the narrowed ranges give €18.2m to €26.1m of FY2026 adjusted EBITDA; less the €20.4m delivered in nine months, the fourth quarter lies between €(2.2)m and €5.7m, a 1.6% margin at the midpoints, matching Deutsche Bank's "below 2%" (4-Nov, via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026).

Against the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (3-Nov): its FY2026 EBITDA forecast of €18.3m sits at the floor of the narrowed guidance; consensus (€21.65m) sits near the middle. The memo forecast "three downgrade steps", the first an FY2026 EBITDA cut "of 10% or more by February"; so far the cut since 3-Nov is 2.3%.

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". Alfen fails it on EBITDA, the line its analysts and its guidance use: FY2027 EBITDA is down 9.0% over 3 months and 3.2% over 6, FY2028 down 6.5% and 2.2%. Only revenue and the noisy FY2027 EPS line are rising.

The SP has fallen twice as fast as the estimates over three months ((18.4%) against (9.0%) for FY2027 EBITDA). Hypothesis: it is pricing the memo's further downgrade steps. Test: whether €9.9 holds through the next round of cuts.

4. Consensus by named FY

Consensus has not moved since the previous run (the latest estimates are of 8-Nov, before the update): analysts keep raising Theon's revenue and EBITDA for every year while cutting FY2027 and FY2028 EPS, and the SP has followed the EPS line.

  • FY2027: EPS down 3.9% over 1 and 3 months and 5.4% over 12; revenue up 1.7%, 2.3% and 13.0%; EBITDA up 3.1%, 5.1% and 17.6%.
  • FY2028: EPS down 2.6% over 1 month and 3.6% over 3; revenue up 2.7% and EBITDA 4.2% over 3 months. FY2026: EPS up 10.3% over 3 months; revenue €601.9m, on guidance of about €600m.

The trajectory: EPS is pulled into FY2026 and pushed out to FY2028, while the operating lines grow evenly. Implied growth from FY2026 to FY2027 is 2.3% for EPS against 25.1% for revenue and 27.1% for EBITDA; from FY2027 to FY2028 it is 19.6% for EPS against 17.0% and 17.9%. The implied EBITDA margin edges up from 27.0% (FY2026) to 27.4% (FY2027) and 27.6% (FY2028). The gap opens below EBITDA: the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (5-Nov) puts it to interest on net debt peaking at about €575m (2.9 times) in mid-2027 after the HGH payment, and calls leverage "the hinge".

Against your rule: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule" (your 3-Nov note, "[PM] Market + FfF; Sector price trends @ 03-Nov-26"). Theon fails it on FY2027 EPS (down 3.9% over 3 months and 2.9% over 6) and passes it on revenue and EBITDA. On 28-Oct you wrote: "Exit unless we can prove strong upgrade scope". The memo expects negative EPS revisions in most months until the leverage question closes, so that proof is unlikely before the FY2026 results (19-Mar-2027).

The test the update sets (memo, 5-Nov): "the nine-month book-to-bill at or above 1.0x is the number that matters"; revenue of about €132m (+38%), a company-basis margin near 23.5% and working capital near its seasonal 45% peak, neither of the last two a miss. The memo puts guidance held at 80%, tightened to "at least 600" at 15% and cut at 5%, and net debt has printed worse than consensus at both 2026 prints.

Every analyst still rates Theon a Buy (7 of 7, 8-Nov), and the ratings have followed valuation, not momentum: Buys rose from 5 to 7 at the November 2025 month-end as the SP fell 17.9%. One Buy left the count after the October month-end with no downgrade recorded; the cause is not in the sources read.

Theon's average PT (€40.14, 8-Nov) is 43.0% above the 9-Nov close, from 50.1% at the 6-Nov close. The gap reached about 35% or more at three month-ends in the last 12 months, not two. After two of them the SP rose by about a quarter: November 2025 (36.6%; +27.6% to 8-Dec-2025) and June 2026 (35.7%; +28.4% to 14-Aug-2026). After the latest, September 2026 (37.3%: average PT €39.48 against the €28.76 close), Theon rose at most 6.5% (to €30.64 on 29-Oct) before making the lower low of 6-Nov: the most recent precedent is the one that failed. The difference now: the gap is wide because the SP fell while the average PT drifted up (€39.37 on 8-Oct to €40.14) from a shrinking count (8 PTs, from 10 in August), with SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth negative: a change of sample, not a vote of confidence.

Net Upgrade Breadth splits in two: positive and rising on the operating lines, negative and falling on EPS and PT (8-Nov, unchanged since the previous run).

  • Rising: FY2027 EBITDA +71% (5 raising, none cutting, of 7), from +50% at the October month-end; FY2027 revenue +43% (3 of 7), from +25%.
  • Falling: FY2027 EPS (29%) (2 raising, 4 cutting, of 7), from (14%) at the October month-end and 0% from June to September; PT (12%) (1 raising, 2 cutting, of 8), after (25%) at October's month-end and +78% at May's.

Where analysts are cutting, and their stated logic: the balance sheet and the multiple, not the order book.

  • PTs: ING (15-Sep) cut to €38 from €42.50 on a 2027 enterprise value to EBIT ratio of 16.1 times, "citing investor sensitivity to leverage", benchmarked to land-defence peers whose multiple fell from 21.6 to 17.2 times, while raising its forecasts; Deutsche Bank put the discount down to "leverage overhang and lower free float rather than order book weakness" (Researcher's report of 29-Oct, in the Sector Digest), and kept Buy and €39 on 2-Nov. The second PT cutter is not named in the sources read.
  • FY2027 EPS: Deutsche Bank trimmed EPS 5% for interest after the 7-Sep report (earnings review); the memo names UBS as carrying interest and amortisation in full (FY2027 EPS €1.42, the lowest). The four analysts cutting now are not named in the sources read.

This breadth carries the sell test SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50, a sell signal at the 30-Oct month-end (SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth (25%), Stock RS 32). Would end it at 30-Nov: SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth back to zero (one PT raise with no new cut) or Stock RS of 50. Test: the PT changes after the 9-Nov update.

9. Peers

Over the fortnight Theon fell with its cohort and rose with it; it fell further and bounced no further. From 29-Oct to 9-Nov Theon fell 8.4%, Exosens 7.7%, Hensoldt 5.5%, MilDef 2.6% and Kongsberg Gruppen 0.9%, with Rheinmetall down 19.3% (chart files); on 9-Nov Theon's +4.9% was mid-pack. On 28-Oct you wrote: "Really, to own Theon is to underwrite the defence trade. What is going on there?" This fortnight's answer: Theon's daily moves are Defence's, amplified, in both directions.

Your four peer checks, for the week to 9-Nov:

  • Newsflow: Rheinmetall's guidance cut and the Boxer delay (5-Nov; item 1 (SP and MAs)); Invisio, a soldier-equipment peer, missed on third-quarter revenue and EBITDA on component costs and US tariffs while order intake surged to SEK 566m (RedEye, 3-Nov, via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026): orders strong, margins squeezed. Nothing in the sources read covers 9-Nov.
  • Company guidance and communications: Rheinmetall now guides 2026 sales of about €13.2bn, from €13.7bn to €14.2bn, its second cut this year (Euronews, 5-Nov-2026). Hensoldt reported its nine months on 5-Nov and holds a capital markets day in London on 10-Nov (Börse Express, 22-Oct-2026); what it reported is not in the sources read.
  • Sell-side estimates (3-month EPS revisions): MilDef +24%, Exosens +6%, Kongsberg Gruppen +4%, Hensoldt +3%; Theon's (4%) is the cohort's second weakest, ahead of Ovzon's (12%).
  • SP technicals: Stock RS 97 at MilDef, 78 at Exail Technologies and 67 at Kongsberg Gruppen (33 a month ago), all above their 50D and absolute 200D MAs; Hensoldt (27) has regained both, 2.8% above its 50D; Exosens (24, from 49 a month ago) sits with Theon (16), 6.8% below its 50D and 10.9% below its absolute 200D MA.

Who turned first: down, Hensoldt and Exosens from 30-Oct, then Rheinmetall on 5-Nov; up, Hensoldt again, which is back above its 50D MA while Theon is 5.9% below its own. From 8-Oct to 9-Nov Kongsberg Gruppen rose 19.9%, MilDef 18.0%, Ovzon 15.1% and Hensoldt 10.9%, against Theon's 3.1% and Exosens' (2.5%): Theon and Exosens are the cohort's laggards on the October to November rebound.

Exosens is Theon's closest match on daily moves (average correlation 0.56, strong), and the link is in the business: Theon holds about 9.8% of Exosens, and Exosens supplies about 75% of Theon's tube spend while Theon is about 40% of Exosens' revenue (Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo).

Hypothesis for Theon's extra fall: a small, levered member of a de-risking industry, sold harder than the group (2.3 times Defence's fall in the week to 6-Nov: (12.1%) against (5.3%) from the 30-Oct close; Exosens close behind at (9.2%)). The alternative, read-across from German call-off delays to Theon's leverage test, has no source linking Rheinmetall's Boxer delay to Theon's programmes; the memo prices one slipped OCCAR quarter at FY2027 EPS (5.6%) and leverage of 2.67 times.

What the peers imply: Theon's direction rests on Defence until its own update gives it news; a day on which Theon rises while Hensoldt and Exosens fall would be the first sign of decoupling, and 10-Nov, with Theon's update and Hensoldt's capital markets day on the same day, is where it can show. The next peer tests: the Bundestag budget committee (12-Nov) and vote (27-Nov) (Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo, 5-Nov; the vote also in the Industry Digest).

What the rules say for Theon now, and what decides the next step

The findings that matter most:

  • The bounce is the industry's, not Theon's: 9-Nov's +4.9% was 1.26 times Defence's +3.9%, on 1.04x volume, and stopped below the falling 20D MA; the broken trend of 5 and 6-Nov stands (items 1, 2 and 9).

  • The update is unread: the third-quarter trading update came after the 9-Nov close; its book-to-bill and net debt decide whether the next move is Theon's own (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)).

What the rules say today, tranche by tranche:

  • The whole holding (tranches 1 and 2, 3.60%): four whole-holding sell signals stand, two from the Friday 6-Nov reading (Industry RS 0; the relative 200D MA down 1.53% over the month) and two from the 30-Oct month-end (Sector RS with Stock RS; SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth with Stock RS). Under your one-off in/out link Theon is sold when its replacement is bought, in the order of sale; nothing sells Theon automatically at the 10-Nov session.
  • Tranches 1 (1.69%) and 2 (1.91%), the 30% fall: triggered on 6-Nov, in the release window. Each is released on the first close at or above €30.75 (15% above the lowest close since the trigger, €26.74) or on the 63rd trading day (about 5-Feb-2027), and sells at the Tuesday session after release even though Theon is under your one-off in/out link. The 9-Nov close (€28.06) is 8.8% below €30.75; a 9.6% rise would reach it.

How the tests interact: the replacement purchase (your link) and the release (the 30% test) are two routes to the same sale, and the earlier decides. The 30% test sells on strength: an update that lifts Theon to a close at or above €30.75 on 10-Nov would sell both tranches at the 17-Nov session.

The nearest test not yet giving a sell signal: relative price more than 25% below its 52W high, read at the 30-Nov month-end. It reads (28.2%) on 9-Nov, against (23.3%) a week earlier, (26.3%) four weeks earlier and (15.6%) thirteen weeks earlier. At a flat market Theon must close at or above about €29.31 (4.5% higher) on 30-Nov to keep it clear. Short of that, a sell signal is more likely than not, which would make five whole-holding sell signals. At the Friday 13-Nov reading the relative 200D MA sell signal will stand (relative price would have to roughly double in four sessions to stop its fall); Industry RS needs 30, from 13.

The record: when Industry RS was last this low, the first Friday reading below 30 was 26-Jun (Industry RS 7, from 33 on 19-Jun); the rule would have sold at the 30-Jun session (€29.84), the day tranche 2 was bought, missing the 28.4% rise to €38.32 (14-Aug), and that sale would have been 6.3% above today's close. The 8-Oct low came within 1.5% of the 30% level before a 12.6% rally; this time the level broke.

What the rules say for Bally's Intralot now, and what decides the next step

The findings that matter most

  • The fundamental work now agrees with the rules: the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo published on 9-Nov concludes "a dated watch-list case, not a current position", rates four Master Ratings F, and gives a 65% chance of a guidance cut on 27-Nov, with a 4.0 times covenant on the Greek retail bond against 4.05 times reported, and the covenant waiver expired on 8-Oct.

Against your 3-Nov rule, "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule", Avanza passes on the mean (FY2027 EPS +1.8% over three months, +7.6% over six) and fails on breadth (8 cutting against 6 raising on EPS).

9. Peers

The cohort has flipped by geography since October: the Nordic and Italian brokers lead the bounce and the United Kingdom platforms now lag. Since the 23-Oct low Fineco is up 10.9%, Nordnet 10.2% and Avanza 8.4%, against Integrafin +2.8%, Flatex +2.7%, Swissquote (2.8%) and AJ Bell (3.3%). In October it was the reverse: AJ Bell and Integrafin rose while Avanza, Nordnet and Fineco fell. The PMS Per-stock Dashboard's peer table shows the levels.

  • SP technicals: Nordnet, whose daily moves track Avanza's most closely (correlation 0.70), is 4.1% above its 50D MA, 3.2% above its 150D and 7.6% above its absolute 200D; Fineco 3.3%, 2.5% and 6.0%. Avanza, at 0.6%, 1.4% and 4.5%, has caught up on the MAs it lagged on 6-Nov, but is still the last of the three. AJ Bell is now below its 50D and 150D MAs ((3.7%) and (3.4%)); Swissquote (13.9% below its absolute 200D) and Flatex (30.0% below) remain broken.
  • Stock RS: Nordnet 67 (60 a month ago), Fineco 49 (45), Avanza 48 (51), Integrafin 48 (55), AJ Bell 47 (66), Swissquote 6, Flatex 2. Fineco and Nordnet turned first; AJ Bell is turning down fastest.
  • Sell-side estimates: Avanza's revenue Net Upgrade Breadth ((19%)) is the cohort's second-worst, ahead only of Flatex ((45%)); Nordnet (+6%), Fineco (+14%) and AJ Bell (+54%) are positive. Three-month FY2027 EPS revisions are +2% at Avanza, Fineco and Nordnet. Avanza's price is catching up with peers whose estimates are better than its own.
  • Newsflow (Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026): Nordnet's October statistics (press release, 4-Nov) showed 26,900 new customers and 5,547,300 trades, "a strong start to Q4" for Deutsche Bank (4-Nov); Deutsche Bank had trimmed Nordnet's EPS 1% on a commission shortfall (Hold, PT SEK 360, 2-Nov). Fineco reported net profit up 10.6% (press release, 5-Nov). No peer news for 9-Nov is in the sources read.

On the key days: on 4-Nov the cohort rose 1.8% on average on heavy volume; on 9-Nov Nordnet and Avanza rose together on light volume while AJ Bell fell 1.9%. Creades, outside the cohort (correlation 0.44) and owner of about 10% of Avanza (the memo), rose 0.8%.

Hypothesis, ranked first: the Swedish rate rise and the October statistics have rotated money within the cohort toward the platforms that earn on client cash in kronor and euros (Avanza, Nordnet, Fineco) and away from the United Kingdom platforms. For: the split began on 4-Nov, the day of the hike; AJ Bell's Stock RS has fallen 19 points in a month. Against: the United Kingdom platforms earn on client cash too, and the cause of 9-Nov is not in the sources read. Test: Avanza and Nordnet against AJ Bell and Integrafin on the cohort's next heavy-volume day.

What the peers imply for Avanza: its next move follows Nordnet, and Nordnet is above every MA with positive breadth. Sector RS (43) needs 7 points to reach 50 at 30-Nov, and the path runs through Fineco and Nordnet, against sector members at Stock RS 2 to 10 (Flatex, IG Group, Swissquote, Plus500). A continued Nordic and Italian rally could clear the Sector RS with Stock RS test without Avanza's own estimates improving.

CVS Group's SP has risen 16.8% in a month on estimates that rose about 1%: the price is leading, and the estimates have not followed.

  • EPS: FY2027 95.6p, +1.1% over 1 month, 0.0% over 3 and 12; FY2028 104.1p, +0.9% over 1 month, (0.7%) over 3, (1.4%) over 12; FY2029 113.1p, +1.6% over 1 month, (3.4%) over 3.
  • Revenue and EBITDA: FY2027 £751.0m and £150.8m (+0.4% and +0.3% over 1 month); FY2028 £785.3m and £159.1m (+0.6% each); FY2029 £823.8m and £167.5m (+2.1% and +1.1%, but EBITDA (3.7%) over 6 months).

The trajectory has not been raised: the later years' EPS rose faster over one month but is lower over three and six months, and FY2029 EBITDA is lower over three and six; revenue for the later years is higher. Consensus implies EPS growth of 8.8% from FY2027 to FY2028 and 8.6% from FY2028 to FY2029; EBITDA growth of 5.5% and 5.3% over the same two steps; revenue growth of 4.6% and 4.9%. EPS grows about 1.6 times as fast as EBITDA, which fits the buyback shrinking the share count. The implied EBITDA margin is 20.1%, 20.3% and 20.3%, against 19.9% in FY2026 and the 19% to 23% target.

Against your rule: your 3-Nov market note says "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". CVS Group still fails it: FY2027 EPS is flat over 3 months, and FY2028 and FY2029 EPS are lower over 3 and 6 months.

The link to the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (3-Nov): it expects the late-November, January and 4-Mar-2027 statements to show group like-for-like growth of about 1.5% against 2.7% a year earlier, and FY2027 EPS consensus to drift to "about 92p to 94p by March", 2% to 4% below today's 95.6p. Implication: the memo expects the 25-Nov trading update to read as deceleration after a 20.2% rise from the 2-Oct low.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

The sell-side is leaning against CVS Group's SP: the average PT has fallen while the SP rose, and a Buy has gone.

  • The average PT was 1,640p on 8-Oct, 1,626p on 14 and 23-Oct, 1,645p on 2-Nov and 1,605p since 3-Nov, a fall of 2.5% that day; the PT has been cut 2% over 1 month and 4% over 3 months on the dashboard's count.
  • Ratings went from 9 Buys and 2 others of 11 at the October month-end to 8 Buys and 3 others of 11 on 8-Nov; no analyst rates CVS Group a Sell.
  • Ratings follow valuation more than momentum: Buys rose after falls (March 2025, July 2026) and slipped at highs (February 2026), as now.

The gap between CVS Group's average PT and its SP is 17.2%, the narrowest since the August 2023 month-end, when the average PT of 2,428p stood 14.6% above the SP of 2,119p. It was 22.5% at 30-Oct and about 44% at the 2-Oct low. Precedent: after the August 2023 gap CVS Group fell 23.1% in the following month (to 1,631p at the September 2023 month-end) and 54.0% by the March 2024 month-end (975p); after the May 2025 gap (21.0%) it rose on the Competition and Markets Authority's provisional decision: news, not the gap, set the direction. Jefferies' 1,400p (Hold) is 2.2% above the 9-Nov close.

Net Upgrade Breadth splits: analysts raise revenue and cut PTs and EPS.

  • PT: (27%), none raising and 3 cutting of 11, unchanged since 3-Nov; (18%) at 30-Oct, 0% at 30-Sep and +20% in July and August; the lowest in the 14 readings since October 2025.
  • Revenue: +33% (3 of 9 raising, none cutting), unchanged since 30-Oct. EPS (FY2027): (10%) (1 raising, 2 cutting, of 10). EBITDA (FY2027): +11% (1 of 9 raising).

Who is cutting, and why: the three PT-cutting brokers are not named in the sources read, and no new source was read this run. The stated logic on file is Jefferies' (Hold, 1,400p; earnings review, 24-Sep): investors will await the new chief executive, and Healthy Pet Club membership falling to 508,000 "highlights structural volume pressure" that would force "downward revisions to FY27 forecasts" without a United Kingdom volume recovery. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (3-Nov) reaches the same place: "the sell-side has bought the re-rating (73 percent Buy, median target 1,600p) without the estimates to support it, so the near-term breadth of revisions tips negative."

Relative price is 27.4% below its 52W high (3-Jun) and 6.5% below its relative 200D MA, which it crossed on 5-Nov for the first time since 2-Feb-2026. Its 6-Nov close (19.99) was the lowest since 14-Jul and undercut the 29-Jul low (20.17), above which every pullback from August to 20-Oct had held; 9-Nov rose 1.7%, less than the SP's 2.7% because the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.3%.

Hexatronic's 1.49x was the heaviest up-day volume in the group that day: a little demand of its own.

Confirms a turn: a Hexatronic close above the absolute 200D MA (37.44) on 1.5x the 50D average volume or more (2.11m shares), in the top third of the range, followed by a session that holds above it. Confirms the move continues: a heavy-volume close below 36.41 (the 6-Nov low close), or any close below 35.57 (5% below the absolute 200D MA).

  • Your rule in the same note ("We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule") is met: Hexatronic's FY2027 EPS is up 10.9% over 3 months and 14.6% over 6 months

Analysts lifted the near year faster than the far year after the 22-Oct report: earnings pulled forward, not a higher trajectory.

On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "FX is likely to be a big headwind, similar to Xvivo - major fundamental risk; read the memo". 2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Basis: one mechanical-volume day, the 18-Sep index review, sits in Helios Towers' 50D average; the ratios below are calculated from the daily volumes without that day, with the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's figure in brackets.

Helios Towers' rebound from the 30-Oct low is six up closes in a row, four of them accumulation days including Monday: the buying that began on 2-Nov has not paused.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Hilton Food against your trend-health tests (applied to the (0.7%) move from the 5-Nov closing high)

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes; up 4.0% over 20 sessions.
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: passes (600.9p against 577.3p).
  • The 50D MA above the 150D and absolute 200D MAs: passes (705.0p).
  • The SP above each: passes; 2.1% above the nearest, the 20D.
  • Near its 52W high and away from its low: passes; 0.7% below the 744.0p closing high, 62.2% above the 455.7p low.
  • The largest one-day or one-week fall since the advance began: passes; the pullback from 5-Nov has fallen at most (1.2%) in a day (6-Nov), against the advance's largest one-day fall, (5.2%) on 28-May, and its largest five-session fall, (9.4%) to 1-Jun.
  • Wider swings: passes; closing pullbacks narrowed from 3.3% (30-Sep to 5-Oct) to 1.5% (2 to 3-Nov) and 1.2% (5 to 6-Nov).
  • Lower lows: passes; intraday lows rose from 721.0p (6-Nov) to 730.0p (9-Nov).
  • More down days than up days: passes; 13 up and 7 down in the last 20 sessions.
  • A light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume: partly; the 4-Nov breakout came on 1.31x the 50D average, below 1.5x, but no heavy-volume reversal has followed (6-Nov fell on 0.43x).
  • The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: partly; in 13 of the last 25 sessions Hilton Food traded below its 20D MA intraday and closed above it, a pattern of the MA rising into the SP.
  • Verdict: a healthy trend, with a breakout still unproven. What would settle it: a close above 755.0p (the 5 and 6-Nov intraday high) on 1.5x the 50D average confirms the breakout; a close back below 734.0p, the base's top, on heavy volume makes it a failed one.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Basis: every multiple of the 50D average volume leaves out the 18-Sep index-review and option-expiry day; the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's figure is in brackets.

The heavy buying of the last 25 sessions all came on 13 to 19-Oct, inside the base; nothing since has reached 1.5x the 50D average.

  • 13-Oct, +1.5% on 602,397 shares (£4.30m), 3.20x (3.14x), closing at 0.94 of the range; 14-Oct, +0.1% on 425,369 shares (£3.04m), 2.27x (2.23x), at 0.50; 15-Oct, (0.7%) on 308,992 shares (£2.19m), 1.63x (1.60x), at 0.15, the period's only distribution day; 19-Oct, +0.4% on 475,848 shares (£3.42m), 2.42x (2.38x), at 0.88. None gapped more than 0.3%. 13 and 14-Oct coincided with Greencore's FY2026 profit-guidance raise and Greencore's own heavy volume (2.7x and 2.1x); 15-Oct with Newtyn Management's holding falling from 5.00% to 4.99% (stock exchange announcement, 20-Oct); 19-Oct was Hilton Food's alone, cause not in the sources read.
  • The breakout, 4-Nov: 255,463 shares (£1.90m), 1.31x (1.28x), closing at the day's high; 9-Nov: 136,999 shares (£1.01m), 0.71x (0.69x).

Accumulation days outnumber distribution days 4 to 1 in 25 sessions (13, 14 and 19-Oct and 4-Nov against 15-Oct); the distribution count was 1 at 12-Oct, 2 at 26-Oct and 2-Nov, and 1 now. Up-day volume divided by down-day volume is 1.60 over 20 sessions (1.91 at 6-Nov) and 2.12 over 5 sessions (the dashboard's figures). Over the whole advance from 29-Jan the average up-day volume is 1.13 times the average down-day volume (216,784 against 192,134 shares), against 0.79 in the 2025 decline from 905.1p; on that basis the last 20 sessions read 1.85, so recent up days carry more volume than the advance's.

Volume has dried up through the base and the breakout: the last 10 sessions averaged 121,755 shares, 0.62x the 50D average (the dashboard's volume trend). Hypothesis: sellers are absent rather than buyers present. For it, the falls of 3 and 6-Nov came on 0.66x and 0.43x. Against it, no up day since 19-Oct has reached 1.5x.

A mechanical change this week: the heavy sessions of 1 to 4-Sep, the 3-Sep results day (1.03M shares) among them, leave the 50D window between 10 and 13-Nov. If Hilton Food keeps trading at its 10-session average, its 50D average (the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's basis, with 18-Sep in) falls from 197,308 shares to about 171,900 by Fri 13-Nov (13% lower), so 1.5x becomes about 258,000 shares (about £1.9m at 739p). The 4-Nov breakout volume would then read about 1.5x: the same trading looks heavier from Friday, a quirk of the window, not new buying.

Precedent: how Hilton Food's earlier bases ended. May, the failure: the 19-May breakout (+2.7% on 1.76x) reversed on 28-May ((5.2%) on 2.15x), and the SP fell 9.4% in five sessions to 1-Jun. August, the success: the base dried up to 0.42x on 26-Aug, then broke out on rising volume (27-Aug 1.00x, 28-Aug 1.58x) before the 3-Sep results added 12.2% on 5.92x. Today's base resembles August's in the volume dry-up and the absence of a heavy reversal, but without August's rising volume on the breakout days.

Hilton Food's relative price has not confirmed the SP's 5-Nov high: on 5-Nov it stood 0.6% below its 8-Oct 52W high and on 9-Nov it is 1.6% below, 20.7% above its relative 200D MA, which rose 3.26% over the month to 9-Nov (3.23% at the 6-Nov reading). On 9-Nov the SP rose 0.5% while the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.3%, and the data file's relative price slipped 0.5% (its benchmark rose about 1.0% that day): on a risk-on day, a low-beta stock (beta 0.35) loses ground relatively without anyone selling it.

Peers' volume: on 9-Nov LDC traded 1.67x and Premier Foods 2.47x their 50D averages (+0.6% and +1.0%); the other cohort peers traded 0.4x to 1.0x. On 13 to 19-Oct only Greencore and, on 15-Oct, Cranswick (1.8x) matched Hilton Food's volume.

What would confirm the turn up: a close above 755.0p on volume above about 258,000 shares (1.5x the 50D average from 13-Nov), with no heavy-volume reversal after it. What would confirm a failure: a close below 734.0p on 1.5x, or a distribution day that closes below the 20D MA, the first such close since 9-Jul.

3. RS

All three of Hilton Food's RS readings sit far above the Sell Criteria's lines; Food production's Sector RS, the nearest, slipped again on Monday.

  • Hilton Food's Stock RS: 89 on 9-Nov (the same as at the 6-Nov Friday reading), from 87 at the 30-Oct month-end and 90 a month ago; 81 or more every Friday since 4-Sep, against lines of 70 and 50. Flat for two months: holding, not rising.
  • Food production's Sector RS: 54 on 9-Nov, from 57 at the 6-Nov reading, 64 on 4-Nov and 55 at 30-Oct; 4 points above the line of 50.
  • Consumer staples' Industry RS: 73, unchanged from the 6-Nov reading and equal to the 13-month high of 23-Oct; 43 points above the line of 30.

The order of the turn: Hilton Food first and alone, then the industry, with the sector lagging. Hilton Food's Stock RS rose from 19 (24-Jul) to 84 (4-Sep) while Sector RS stayed between 29 and 43; Industry RS rose from 33 (2-Oct) to 73 (23-Oct). The rotation into staples is now lifting Hilton Food's laggard peers more than Hilton Food (item 9, Peers), and Monday's risk-on day, when Consumer staples rose 0.3% against the Stoxx Europe 600's 1.3%, is the kind of day that would test the Industry Digest's 30-Oct de-grossing hypothesis. On its own test ("staples falling on days Industrials rise supports de-grossing") Monday leans against de-grossing: Industrials and capital goods rose 1.5% and Consumer staples rose rather than fell; and the Digest's other test, Industry RS above 50 at the 30-Oct and 6-Nov readings (66 and 73), has passed.

Precedent: Food production's Sector RS had three earlier spells above 50 this year: six Fridays (6-Mar to 10-Apr), five (24-Apr to 22-May) and two (10 and 17-Jul). The current spell is four Fridays (16-Oct to 6-Nov). Test, Fri 13-Nov: a fifth Friday above 50. Sector RS has fallen 10 points in three sessions (from 64 on 4-Nov to 54 on 9-Nov); it is the reading most likely to cross its line first, and it matters for the Pool of Stocks Eligible for Middle Innings Portfolio Selection (closing paragraph).

4. Consensus by named FY

Hilton Food's EPS upgrades have stalled for a month, and the later FYs rise more slowly than the nearer one. (Financial year to December; sterling. No consensus figure changed between the 6-Nov run and the latest data, 8-Nov.)

  • EPS: FY2026 52.2p, +1.5% over 1 month, +6.9% over 3, +9.7% over 6; FY2027 56.9p, +1.0%, +6.1%, +8.1%; FY2028 62.1p, (0.7%), +2.9%, +3.9%. No FY has moved more than 0.1% since 8-Oct.
  • The shape: FY2026 has risen fastest and FY2028 slowest over every period, so analysts are lifting the near year (the 3-Sep guidance raise, which Shore Capital called a technical adjustment, per the earnings review) rather than the trajectory; implied EPS growth is 9.0% into FY2027 and 9.1% into FY2028.
  • Revenue and EBITDA: FY2027 revenue £4,798m (+1.3% over 1 month, +5.4% over 3), largely raw-material prices passed through at cost; FY2026 to FY2028 EBITDA £149.8m, £160.5m and £168.2m (+4.3%, +3.4% and +2.5% over 1 month). Implied EBITDA margins are 3.25%, 3.35% and 3.39%. The previous note judged the month's EBITDA rise (the same £4.2m in each FY on 4-Nov, with EPS unmoved) one analyst changing basis, not an upgrade; nothing since changes that.

Hilton Food's SP has run far ahead of its estimates: +40.3% over 6 months against FY2027 EPS +8.1%, so the multiple did most of the work. Your 3-Nov market note's rule ("We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule") is met over 3 and 6 months; over 1 month the upgrades have stopped.

On 28 to 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "IMHO the case needs another fundamental leg of delivery for SP to power onwards or it'll retrace hard". No new leg has arrived, and the estimates have not moved since 8-Oct. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (4-Nov) dates the next legs: the NADEC joint venture in Saudi Arabia (fourth quarter of 2026), the Polish capacity decision (end-2026) and Walmart Canada (January 2027).

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Hilton Food's ratings and PTs follow its SP: the analysts upgraded after the rise, not after the falls. Buys stood at 4 or 5 through most of the 2025 decline (apart from 2 at the August 2025 month-end, when only two analysts gave a rating) and settled at 3 only after the 11-Nov-2025 profit warning, when the average PT fell 32% in a month (1,071p to 730p). Buys fell again to 2 at the July and August 2026 month-ends as the SP rose and coverage fell from 6 to 4, then rose to 4 after the 3-Sep results. All 4 analysts with a rating say Buy (8-Nov).

The gap between the average PT and the SP has closed with the advance: 44.6% at the January 2026 month-end, 29.5% at June, 3.9% at September, and 7.6% now (795p against 739.0p). The average has been 795p since 8-Oct; the latest named PT is Deutsche Bank's 850p, reiterated with Buy on 5-Nov (Deutsche Bank Research, via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026).

Net Upgrade Breadth is positive on every measure, but one analyst is cutting FY2027 EPS and no new raise has come since early October.

  • EPS (FY2027): +40% (3 raising, 1 cutting, of 5), from +60% (3 raising, none cutting) at the July and August month-ends. EBITDA +60% (3 raising, none cutting); revenue +40% (2 raising, none cutting, of 5); PT +50% (2 raising, none cutting, of 4). Unchanged since the 30-Oct month-end.
  • Where analysts are cutting: the sources read do not name the analyst cutting FY2027 EPS or give the logic. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo names the visible brokers as Deutsche Bank, Peel Hunt and Shore Capital and puts Peel Hunt's 720p PT "at the price"; Peel Hunt is the likeliest candidate, but that is an inference, not a source.
  • The drift: the raises after the 3-Sep results leave the 100-day window in which Net Upgrade Breadth counts revisions in December, so without new raises revenue and PT Net Upgrade Breadth will drift towards zero ahead of the 31-Dec month-end reading of the SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth test.

9. Peers

Hilton Food led its cohort out of the trough; over the past month the laggards caught up and Hilton Food was the cohort's slowest riser. (The table and chart are on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard.)

  • SP technicals: over the month to 9-Nov Greencore rose 18.2%, Cranswick 10.9%, LDC 9.0%, Bell Food Group 6.2% and Hilton Food 3.6%; all five are above their 50D MAs.
  • RS: over the month Cranswick's Stock RS rose from 31 to 55, LDC's from 39 to 53, Greencore's from 72 to 79 and Bell Food Group's from 83 to 90, now level with Hilton Food's 89, which slipped from 90.
  • Sell-side estimates: Hilton Food has the cohort's broadest revenue raises (revenue Net Upgrade Breadth +40%, against Greencore +11%, Cranswick and Bell Food Group 0%, LDC (25%)); Bell Food Group has the largest 3-month EPS rise (+10% against Hilton Food's +6%).
  • Newsflow and guidance: the 6-Nov AlphaSense report had nothing material on the four cohort peers; Greencore's 13-Oct FY2026 profit-guidance raise is the cohort's only guidance change since Hilton Food's own of 3-Sep, and Greencore's results on 1-Dec are the cohort's next dated test.

On 28 to 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Greencore and Bell have strong technical momentum vs. Cranswick; it's the lower-quality names "catching a bid" vs. the slow and steady names (risky, but working ATM)". The bid has since broadened to the slow and steady names: Cranswick added 24 points of Stock RS in a month, more than any peer.

What Hilton Food trades with now: Consumer staples as a block. Its big moves of July to September were its own (the Dalco sale, +5.5% on 31-Jul; the results, +12.2% on 3-Sep). This month it moves with Associated British Foods, its most correlated stock outside the cohort (+6.4% on 4-Nov, (1.9%) on 6-Nov, +0.6% on 9-Nov, against Hilton Food's +2.8%, (1.2%) and +0.5%).

What the peers imply. Hypothesis 1 (ranked first): a rotation into the whole staples group that lifts the laggards most and carries Hilton Food along; for it, Industry RS at 73 and Hilton Food's daily moves matching the industry's. Hypothesis 2: money leaving the leader for the laggards; for it, Hilton Food's flat Stock RS and its lag over the month; against it, only one distribution day (15-Oct) in 25 sessions. Test, by Fri 13-Nov: Hilton Food falling on days Cranswick and LDC rise favours the second; Food production's Sector RS, carried by these peers, is the Hilton Food reading nearest its line.
