Carlsberg has given back 1.4% in two sessions from its 52W closing high, and Monday 9-Nov was the first day since the 2-Nov breakout that the market rose and Carlsberg did not. Carlsberg closed at DKK 1,017.5 on 9-Nov, down 0.8%, 1.4% below the DKK 1,031.5 closing high of 5-Nov and 3.6% above the DKK 981.8 ceiling of 12-Feb. That day the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.3%, led by Defence (+3.9%), Industrials and capital goods (+1.5%) and Energy, commodities and metals mining (+1.5%), while Consumer staples rose 0.3%: a one-day rotation back towards cyclicals, in which Carlsberg's relative price fell 1.8%.

The trend is intact over every period, and the week was quiet. Carlsberg is up 1.2% over the week from the 2-Nov close, 16.4% over 1 month, 11.7% over 3 and 37.0% over 12. With Carlsberg's beta at 0.87, the Stoxx Europe 600's 1.1% week explains 0.9 points of the 1.2%, 0.1 times Carlsberg's usual weekly move; over 20 sessions Carlsberg's own part is 10.8 points, 1.6 times its usual 20-session move. The October advance, not this week, is the large move.

Developments. Richard's weekly AlphaSense report for this week was not read (the AlphaSense sign-in form showed and no Chrome browser was connected); the Developments come from the report of 6-Nov-2026, the earnings review and the data file. The cause of Carlsberg's 9-Nov fall is not in the sources read; it matches the day's move from defensives into Defence, Industrials and capital goods, and Energy. Carlsberg's section of the 6-Nov report holds only Morgan Stanley's NielsenIQ scanner note of 30-Oct (beer volumes in Great Britain and France "under pressure", "offset slightly by pricing"; no change to Carlsberg's PT or rating) and "no new regulatory filings" (via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026). Carlsberg's last company event was its third-quarter trading statement (reaction day 29-Oct), which reiterated FY2026 organic operating profit growth guidance of 4% to 6% (earnings review).

Hypotheses for the dip, ranked by evidence: 1. A pause in the defensive rotation, not Carlsberg-specific selling. For it: 9-Nov's fall came on 0.81x volume with a mid-range close, and every brewer fell or lagged. Against it: on 9-Nov Carlsberg fell about 0.6 points more than AB InBev and Heineken ((0.2%) each), and on 6-Nov it fell (0.5%) with Heineken (0.5%) while AB InBev rose 0.1%. For it again: Royal Unibrew, the brewer that tracks Carlsberg most closely, fell more on both days ((2.0%) on 6-Nov, (1.0%) on 9-Nov). 2. The start of the retrace you expected. On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "SP has popped hard like L2W in Feb-26 and Jul-26 (both times after earnings…?) - chances are it retraces somewhat here". For it: Carlsberg's July top also began with light-volume down days. Against it: 1.4% is under half the usual weekly move of 3.3%.

Test: a further light-volume dip shared by the brewers favours the first; a heavy-volume Carlsberg fall closing near its low while AB InBev and Heineken hold would turn the second into distribution.

The MAs: all seven rise over 20 sessions; only the 100D MA is still above the faster 50D MA. The order is 5D (DKK 1,020.30), 10D (992.92), 20D (969.46), 100D (909.65), 50D (905.52), 150D (887.85), absolute 200D (885.83). The 150D MA has led the absolute 200D MA since 3-Nov (by DKK 2.02), and the 50D MA has narrowed its gap to the 100D MA from DKK 5.12 (6-Nov) to DKK 4.13. No 20D, 50D, 100D, 150D or absolute 200D MA has been crossed since 12-Oct. At Carlsberg's February and July tops no retest of the 50D, 100D or 150D MA held.

The 20D MA is rising to meet the SP. At a flat SP of DKK 1,017.5 it would reach about DKK 981 (the old ceiling) on Wed 11-Nov, DKK 993 by Mon 16-Nov and DKK 1,005 by Mon 23-Nov (my calculation from the chart file). The retest of a rising MA your 2-Nov note waits for can come through time as well as price.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Carlsberg against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising (+1.8% over 20 sessions); the 150D MA above it (by DKK 2.02, narrowly); the 50D MA above both: all pass.
  • The SP above each MA: passes for the 10D to absolute 200D MAs (2.5% to 14.9% above); the 9-Nov close is 0.3% below the 5D MA.
  • Near its 52W high (1.4% below) and away from its low (38.9% above the DKK 732.64 low of 18-Nov-2025): passes.
  • Stage 3 (topping) red flags: the largest one-day and one-week falls since the advance began (29-Oct, (3.0%); 22 to 29-Oct, (4.7%)) came on a mixed statement, not on good news, so this flag is partly present. Absent: wider swings (average daily move 0.99% over 5 sessions against 1.42% over 20: swings are narrowing); lower lows; more down days than up days (22 up, 14 down since 18-Sep, though 3 of the last 5 fell); a light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume; the SP tracking a rising MA.
  • Verdict: a healthy advance in its first, shallow dip, with no retest yet to judge. A close back below DKK 981.8 on above-average volume would be the first reading against it.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Carlsberg's first two down days off its high have come on falling volume, which is how a healthy pullback starts, but also how its July top started. On 9-Nov Carlsberg fell 0.8% on 189,663 shares, DKK 193.0m, 0.81x its 50D average volume (0.83x without mechanical-volume days), after a 0.9% gap down, closing at 0.50 of the day's range. On 6-Nov it fell 0.5% on 1.33x (316,601 shares, DKK 324.8m) but closed at the day's high after a 2.1% gap down. The precedents split: after the 12-Feb high Carlsberg fell 1.8% on 1.67x the next day, closing at 0.26 of its range, and the 20.0% fall followed; after the 30-Jul high volume dried up to 0.50x to 0.66x for six sessions and the 12.9% fall began quietly, breaking the 20D MA on 10-Aug on 1.06x. What separates a pause from July's pattern is what happens at the rising 20D MA, now 4.7% below the close.

Heavy-volume days (1.5x the 50D average or more) in the last 25 sessions: three, none since 30-Oct. 15-Oct, +3.6% on 1.48x (1.54x without mechanical-volume days), DKK 299.9m, closing at 0.89 of the range, a brewers' day; 27-Oct, (1.4%) on 2.07x (2.14x), DKK 445.1m, closing at 0.11, Carlsberg's own before the statement; 30-Oct, +3.9% on 2.43x (2.51x), DKK 551.1m, a 2.2% gap up, the post-statement reversal.

Distribution against accumulation, 25 sessions: 3 distribution days (27 and 29-Oct around the statement, and 6-Nov, whose close at the day's high reads as demand absorbing the market's fall) against 6 accumulation days through the advance (7, 14, 15, 28 and 30-Oct, 2-Nov). The dashboard's count rose from 1 at 2-Nov to 3.

Up-day against down-day volume: 1.06 over 20 sessions and 0.99 over 5 (the dashboard's figures); over the advance from 18-Sep (+21.0%, 36 sessions) 1.04, against 1.26 and 1.16 for the advances to February and May 2026 and 1.02 for June to July 2026, which gave way to the 12.9% fall. Still the advance's weak point, though the 20D average volume has risen to 1.37x the 50D average.

Volume at the MAs: the 150D and absolute 200D MAs were regained on 7-Oct on 1.36x, the 50D and 100D on 12-Oct on only 0.81x, and the DKK 981.8 ceiling on 2-Nov on 1.33x. No MA has been tested since.

Precedent: of Carlsberg's last three lows (24-Mar, 4-Jun, 18-Sep) only March's turned on heavy-volume up days within ten sessions: its turns come quietly. The one mechanical-volume day in 50 sessions (18-Sep) moves the ratios above by 0.03 to 0.07 at most.

Carlsberg's relative price has still not confirmed the SP's high, and lost ground on 9-Nov. It is 6.1% below its 52W high of 12-Feb (4.4% on 6-Nov; 4.6% on the 5-Nov SP high) and 8.3% above its relative 200D MA, which it crossed on 15-Oct. An SP high without a relative-price high marks the advance as partly the market's: confirmation needs about 6.5% more outperformance of the Stoxx Europe 600.

Peers' volume on 9-Nov (my calculation from the chart files): AB InBev 1.18x (closing at its high), Heineken 0.99x, Royal Unibrew 1.12x (closing at its low): no brewer saw heavy selling.

Confirms the advance: a pullback towards DKK 981.8 and the rising 20D MA on volume below the 50D average that holds, then an up day on 1.5x or more closing in its top third. Confirms a failure: a close below DKK 981.8 on above-average volume, then below the 29-Oct low of DKK 934.2 (8.2% below the close).

3. RS

Carlsberg's three RS readings eased from their highs on 9-Nov but all stay in the 70s: Stock RS 78, Beverages' Sector RS 78, Consumer staples' Industry RS 73. Each RS is a percentile rank from 0 to 99.

  • Pace: Carlsberg's Stock RS is 2 points down on the Fri 6-Nov reading (80), 24 up on Fri 9-Oct (54) and 20 up on Fri 14-Aug (58). Beverages' Sector RS is 2 down on 6-Nov and 24 up on 9-Oct; Consumer staples' Industry RS is unchanged on 6-Nov and 27 up on 9-Oct.
  • The order of the turn: Carlsberg's Stock RS and Beverages' Sector RS turned together at the Fri 25-Sep reading, Consumer staples' Industry RS from 9-Oct; Beverages' Sector RS led to 23-Oct (85 against Carlsberg's 73); since the 29-Oct statement Carlsberg's Stock RS has risen faster (59 to 78 against Beverages' 70 to 78), drawing level at the Fri 6-Nov reading. A rotation is carrying the stock.

Precedent: Carlsberg's Stock RS topped at 74 (Fri 13-Feb) and 68 (31-Jul month-end), then fell to 42 and 41 with 20.0% and 12.9% SP falls. Its largest fall within 25 days is 22 points (1 to 15-May-2026); Beverages' Sector RS fell 38 points in 12 days (31-Jul to 12-Aug). Refuted as a top if Stock RS holds 70 or more at the Fri 13-Nov and Fri 20-Nov readings with the SP above DKK 981.8. Peers: Heineken and AB InBev eased from 75 to 72 on 9-Nov; Carlsberg still leads its cohort.

4. Consensus by named FY

Carlsberg's consensus has not moved since 4-Nov; the month's EPS rise is under 1% (revenue and EBITDA up 0.1% to 1.2%), partly currency, so the SP's 16.4% month is a re-rating.

  • The chart's 1, 3 and 6-month changes to 8-Nov: FY2026 EPS DKK 65.18, +0.4%, +0.6% and +0.9%; FY2027 EPS DKK 70.40, +0.8%, (0.2%) and (0.6%); FY2028 EPS DKK 76.78, +0.9%, +0.6% and +0.1%. FY2027 revenue +1.1%, +0.7% and +0.8%; FY2027 EBITDA +0.7%, (1.2%) and (1.3%); FY2028 EBITDA +1.0%, (0.9%) and (1.1%).
  • The trajectory: over the month FY2028 EPS rose 0.9% against 0.4% for FY2026, a slightly raised trajectory; over 12 months the reverse, FY2026 EPS +3.4% and FY2028 EPS (3.5%), earnings pulled forward. Implied EPS growth is 8.0% (FY2026 to FY2027) and 9.1% (FY2027 to FY2028) on revenue growth of 4.6% and 3.6%; the implied EBITDA margin rises from 21.0% (FY2026) to 21.3% (FY2027) and 21.7% (FY2028): a Britvic synergy and soft-drinks case, not a beer volume case. The month's rise follows the FY2026 translation tailwind raised to DKK 100m in the third-quarter statement (earnings review).

On 3-Nov you wrote in your market note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". Carlsberg still fails it on the record: FY2027 EPS is down 0.2% over three months and 0.6% over six, and FY2027 and FY2028 EBITDA are down over both periods; only the last month is up, by under 1%, after the third-quarter statement raised the FY2026 translation tailwind to DKK 100m (the sources read do not say why FY2027 and FY2028 rose more than FY2026). Group beer volume fell 3.5% in the third quarter (earnings review).

Confirms the SP's lead: organic EPS raises before the FY2026 results of 9-Feb-2027. Refutes it: FY2027 EPS back below DKK 70 while the SP holds up.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Carlsberg's average PT, DKK 1,070 (8-Nov, from DKK 1,062 on 2-Nov), is 5.2% above the 9-Nov close; the 9-Nov dip reopened the gap from 4.3% on 6-Nov, still the narrowest against any month-end reading in 24 months. Hypothesis: the average PT acts as a ceiling. For it: after month-end gaps of 5.8% (May 2025), 10.4% (February 2026) and 10.7% (July 2026) Carlsberg fell 12.1% to 19.9% within 63 sessions. Against it: PTs are rising with the SP this time. Test: further PT raises while the SP holds above DKK 1,000.

The latest PT changes (earnings review; Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 4-Nov-2026): Deutsche Bank raised to DKK 1,200 (Buy, 29-Oct) and Bernstein to DKK 1,550 (Outperform, 30-Oct); Morgan Stanley (DKK 1,075) and Rothschild & Co Redburn (DKK 1,100) held; none since 30-Oct in the sources read. Four PTs sit below the 9-Nov close: Barclays DKK 930 (Equal Weight, 12-Oct), Jefferies DKK 950 (Hold, since 13-Sep), HSBC DKK 980 (Hold) and DNB Carnegie DKK 995, 8.6% to 2.2% below.

Ratings: 20 Buys, 8 Holds, no Sells, unchanged since the 31-Oct month-end (21 Buys, 5 Holds, 1 Sell at 31-Aug). The Buy share has fallen from 78% to 71% while the SP rose: as from October 2024 to June 2025, Carlsberg's ratings follow valuation while its PTs follow momentum.

Net Upgrade Breadth is positive on every measure and unchanged since the 5-Nov count: EPS +19% (12 raising, 7 cutting, of 26), EBITDA +59% (14 and 1, of 22), revenue +60% (16 and 1, of 25), PT +32% (12 and 4, of 25), against (32%), (29%), (17%) and (22%) at 30-Sep. The divergence: EPS breadth is the weakest, with 7 analysts cutting EPS while 1 cuts revenue or EBITDA; plausibly cuts below the operating line, though the sources read do not say.

Where analysts are cutting: Rothschild & Co Redburn cut its FY2027 organic revenue growth by 50 basis points to 3.0% (29-Oct) on "persistent consumer weakness in China that is likely to continue through the first half of 2027"; Jefferies has kept Hold at DKK 950 since its 13-Sep downgrade on China, its analyst noting Chinese catering and entertainment channels "failed to recover" (earnings review). The other EPS cutters are not named in the sources read.

9. Peers

Beverages broke out as a group and is now pausing as a group; over the week from 2-Nov Carlsberg is the laggard among the large names, because the week leaves out its own 2-Nov breakout. Over the week to 9-Nov Carlsberg rose 1.2% against AB InBev 2.1%, Heineken 2.3%, Diageo 4.1%, Campari 5.0% and Royal Unibrew (0.4%), with Consumer staples up 1.5% and the Stoxx Europe 600 1.1%. Over the month Carlsberg (+16.4%) is second among the brewers to Heineken (+17.2%), ahead of AB InBev (+10.0%); in Beverages only Rémy Cointreau (+21.6%) and Heineken rose more.

  • SP technicals (my calculation from the chart files): on 9-Nov Coca-Cola Europacific Partners closed at a 52W high and Diageo, AB InBev, Campari, Rémy Cointreau and Heineken 0.1% to 0.8% below theirs; Carlsberg, 1.4% below, is the furthest by under a point. Royal Unibrew, which tracks Carlsberg's daily moves most closely with Heineken (average correlation 0.72 each, the dashboard's measure), is 28.2% below its February high.
  • Who turned first: Carlsberg held its 18-Sep low on 1-Oct while Heineken and AB InBev made lower lows, and its Stock RS has led the cohort since the 29-Oct statement. No peer has yet turned down first; all four brewers eased together on 9-Nov.
  • Sell-side estimates (peer table): AB InBev, Heineken and Royal Unibrew each show three-month EPS revisions of (1%), Carlsberg 0%; only Diageo (+3%) and Rémy Cointreau (+5%) show real raises in Beverages. Carlsberg's revenue Net Upgrade Breadth (+60%) is the cohort's best, against AB InBev's (20%) and Heineken's +15%. The group, like Carlsberg, is rising on price with flat estimates; Carlsberg's breadth is the best of a flat group.

Newsflow and guidance (Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026, and the sources named); nothing newer was read:

  • Heineken reiterated its 2026 guidance on 28-Oct with China "sustained double-digit growth and share gains" (GlobeNewswire, 28-Oct), against Carlsberg's China volume down nearly 10% (earnings review): the China gap is Carlsberg's, not the group's.
  • Diageo beat in its first-quarter trading statement on 5-Nov (North American organic sales (3.6%) against (6.7%) expected; Deutsche Bank, 5-Nov): the spirits "worry clearing" of the Sector Digest is passing its first test.
  • Coca-Cola HBC fell 17.9% on 5-Nov on a Russian request to place its subsidiaries under state management (Dnews, 6-Nov), a day after raising guidance; Carlsberg's Russian loss was taken in 2023, so the read-across is to Beverages' Sector RS only.

What the peers imply for Carlsberg. The brewers rose together and are pausing together, so Beverages' Sector RS, half of Carlsberg's nearest sell test, needs a group-wide fall to approach 50, as at the 31-Aug month-end. The signal to watch: heavy-volume down days across the brewers closing near their lows; 6-Nov's gap-and-recover was the opposite, and 9-Nov's light-volume dip is neither.

What the rules say for Carlsberg now, and what decides the next step

  • Most important: the first dip off the breakout is shallow and quiet: 1.4% below the 5-Nov high, 3.6% above the DKK 981.8 ceiling, on 1.33x the 50D average volume on 6-Nov (closing at the day's high) and 0.81x on 9-Nov (closing mid-range). Only a heavy-volume fall closing near its low, as on 13-Feb, would read as distribution.
  • Second: the SP is still ahead of the sell-side. The average PT is 5.2% above the SP; EPS estimates are up under 1% in a month, partly on currency, and FY2027 EPS is down over three and six months, so your 3-Nov rule on upgrading over the next three to six months is not met on the record.
  • Third: the retest you wait for is close in time, not yet in price. The rising 20D MA is 4.7% below the close and, at a flat SP, reaches about DKK 993 by 16-Nov and DKK 1,005 by 23-Nov.
  • The rules today: hold; no sell signal from any of the six Sell Criteria. The two Friday tests, read on Fri 6-Nov (Consumer staples' Industry RS 73; the relative 200D MA rising 0.79% over the month) stand until Fri 13-Nov, and neither can plausibly turn by then (Industry RS needs a 44-point fall; relative price is 8.3% above its relative 200D MA). All three tranches are held; nothing sells at Tue 10-Nov.
  • The nearest sell test, Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70 (read at the Mon 30-Nov month-end): Carlsberg's Stock RS is 78, 8 points above 70 ((2) since the Fri 6-Nov reading, +8 since the 30-Oct month-end, +24 since Fri 9-Oct, +20 since Fri 14-Aug); Beverages' Sector RS is 78, 28 above 50 ((2) since 6-Nov, +2 since 30-Oct, +24 since 9-Oct, +45 since Fri 14-Aug but +20 since Fri 7-Aug: it swings fast). At 1.1 to 2.2 points of Stock RS per 1% of relative price (9-Nov, and 29-Oct to 6-Nov; a rank, so a guide only), about 4% to 8% of underperformance takes Stock RS below 70: at a flat market, an SP of about DKK 935 to 975, around the DKK 981.8 ceiling. A sell signal also needs Beverages' Sector RS below 50.
  • How the tests interact: a group-wide fall can meet both halves at once, as from the 31-Jul month-end (Stock RS 68, Sector RS 68) to 31-Aug (49 and 47); a Carlsberg-only fall meets only the Stock RS half. The other tests are far: relative price more than 25% below its 52W high needs an SP of about DKK 813 at a flat market (20.1% below); a tranche's 30% fall needs DKK 722.05 (29.0% below), with Stock RS below 70. A whole-holding sell signal would sell Carlsberg when its replacement is bought, under your one-off in/out link.
  • The record: this test's last sell signal came late, at the 31-Aug month-end at DKK 883.4, 8.4% below the 30-Jul high; the SP fell 4.8% more to 18-Sep, then rose 22.7% to 5-Nov.
  • Adds: the rules allow an add. No sell signal, and 2.09% of room below the 6.68% limit on Carlsberg's current 4.59% of capital (3.98% at entry): one 1.67% tranche, and a second of at most 0.42%.
  • Where Carlsberg stands for an add: none of the three stages yet (not pulling back or basing; not retesting a rising MA or forming a volatility contraction pattern; not healthily retesting a rising MA or breaking out): a 1.4% dip is under half the usual weekly move. The Master Dashboard's tests point to pulling back or basing (basing plausible, 3 of 4; pulling back possible, 2 of 4; healthy retest possible, 3 of 6, no MA retested; volatility contraction pattern 0 of 4). My reading: a light-volume dip beyond 3.3% towards DKK 981.8 would be the first stage, a hold at the rising 20D MA the third. No add trigger is present; a broadening of the case is partly present.
  • For your ruling: on 2-Nov you wrote "SUMMARY - Add 2-4% in 2 tranches over N2W hopefully from successful retesting of rising MAs". The room takes 2.09%, the low end of that range; within your two weeks the rising 20D MA is the only MA a pullback can realistically retest (the 50D MA is 11.0% below the close).
  • The dates: FY2026 results on 9-Feb-2027 (confirmed); no Carlsberg event before the 30-Nov month-end in the sources read. What would change this reading: the volume tests at the end of item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high).
  • Your October P&L review (9-Nov): you wrote "Stock selection was also a drag in the month"; Carlsberg was not part of it, rising 14.0% from the 30-Sep to the 30-Oct close, roughly half a point of fund return at its size near 4% (my estimate), with no currency effect under the krone's euro peg.

Richard decides.

D'Ieteren

Early Innings Probing Bet · 2.61% of capital in 1 tranche (tranche 1, 2.61%, bought 12-Aug-2026) · readings as at Mon 9-Nov-2026

1. SP and MAs

On Mon 9-Nov D'Ieteren's SP rose 1.6% to €181.5 on light volume (0.65x), a market day: it does not settle whether the failed 4-Nov breakout met supply from sellers at the top of the range or a pause in the market. The Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.3% and D'Ieteren's beta to it is 1.23, so the market explains about 1.5 points of the 1.6%. The 9-Nov close sits between the two price lines set in the previous note's tests: above €175.9 (which would have favoured supply) and below €182.8, the 4-Nov close (which would have favoured a pause).

  • The trend: over 12 months D'Ieteren is up 19.0%; over 3 months +2.8%, against +1.3% for Consumer discretionary and (0.4%) for the Stoxx Europe 600. Over the week +2.2% (industry +1.6%, market +1.1%); after removing the market's move at D'Ieteren's beta of 1.23 it is +0.9%, which is 0.2 times its usual weekly move of 3.6%.
  • November and October: +5.0% in November (€172.9 to €181.5), the gain your 4-Nov note recorded ("up 5% in Nov-26"). In October D'Ieteren rose 6.1% against the Stoxx Europe 600's +1.9%, so it helped rather than hurt the stock selection your 9-Nov Monthly P&L Review records as a drag; as a euro-listed stock it had no part in the FX boost.
  • The range: since the €195.44 close of 10-Feb the swing highs came lower (€186.06, €184.3, €180.4 on 4-Sep) and the lows rose (€153.86, €158.9, €160.0, €170.4 on 28-Oct); the 4-Nov close broke the line of lower highs, and 5-Nov failed at €187.0 intraday.

Developments: Richard's weekly AlphaSense report was not read this run (the AlphaSense sign-in form showed and no Chrome browser was connected), so nothing in the sources read covers 9-Nov for D'Ieteren, and no research has been filed since 6-Nov. The last report read (6-Nov-2026) found "No material news flow" and "No new sell-side notes" for D'Ieteren, only the weekly liquidity-contract statement (Press Release, 4-Nov).

The legs since the 24-Sep low (closes; industry = Consumer discretionary, equal-weighted):

  • 24-Sep to 16-Oct, +9.9% (industry +3.5%): D'Ieteren's own, led by Bloomberg's report that Belron had picked four banks for a listing (Bloomberg Línea, 8-Oct; +2.2% on 2.72x against an industry down 0.8%). 21 to 28-Oct, (3.1%) (industry (1.0%)): its own, on light volume, with a 1.7% FY2027 EPS cut on 27-Oct.
  • 28-Oct to 4-Nov, +7.3% (industry +4.4%): the industry's leg. 4 to 6-Nov, (2.3%) (industry (0.5%), market (1.4%)): the market's and its own, reversing with Inter Cars and Sixt (item 9 (Peers)).
  • 9-Nov, +1.6% (industry +0.5%, market +1.3%): the market's; Consumer discretionary ranked tenth of 16 industries. The cause beyond the market is not in the sources read.

The €180 to €187 zone has met seven D'Ieteren rallies this year. Of the earlier 2026 sessions when D'Ieteren first closed 6% or more above its 50D MA (it is 6.3% above today), only 9-Jan ran on; after the other five (17-Apr to 4-Aug) it fell 5.2% to 12.6% within 20 sessions. Hypotheses, ranked: (1) range supply: the 5 and 6-Nov down days traded four times the volume of 9-Nov's rebound; (2) a market pause: the market explains all of 9-Nov and about half of 6-Nov. Test: a close above €182.8 on 1.5x the 50D average or more favours the second; a close below €175.9 on above-average volume the first.

The MAs: the SP closes above all five, which sit within 2.7% of one another, near the reverse of a Stage 2 (advancing) order: the 20D (€175.42, +6.5% over 20 sessions), the 100D (€173.40), the absolute 200D (€173.17), the 150D (€172.15) and the 50D (€170.77, (0.5%) over 20 sessions). At a flat SP of €181.5 the 150D passes above the absolute 200D, and the 50D above both, on Tue 24-Nov, as February's €185 to €195 closes leave the absolute 200D's window; the same arithmetic turns the absolute 200D down, to €172.37 by 30-Nov. The order improves by arithmetic, not by new buying.

Healthy retest or broken trend? D'Ieteren against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: partly, rolling over: +0.3% over 20 sessions, (0.1%) over 5; at a flat SP (0.5%) by 30-Nov.
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: fails: 0.6% below; at a flat SP it passes on 24-Nov.
  • The 50D MA above the 150D and absolute 200D MAs: fails: 0.8% and 1.4% below; at a flat SP it passes both on 24-Nov.
  • The SP above each MA: passes: 4.8% above the absolute 200D, 6.3% above the 50D.
  • Near its 52W high: partly: 7.1% below the €195.44 close of 10-Feb.
  • Away from its 52W low: passes: 27.2% above the €142.7 close of 25-Nov-2025.
  • The largest one-day or one-week fall since the advance began: present, mildly: 6-Nov's (2.0%) on 1.18x, with the market explaining more than half.
  • Wider swings: present: daily ranges of 2.1% to 3.7% of the close from 2 to 9-Nov, against 1.2% to 1.3% on 29 and 30-Oct.
  • Lower lows: absent: the 9-Nov low (€178.2) is above the 6-Nov low (€176.8).
  • More down days than up days: absent: 5 and 5 in 10 sessions; 16 up, 15 down and one unchanged (2-Oct, €162.9) since 24-Sep.
  • A light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume: present: the 4-Nov breakout on 1.14x reversed on 1.49x and 1.18x.
  • The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: no longer: five crossings of the absolute 200D from 13-Oct to 2-Nov, none since.
  • Verdict: a stalled breakout inside an intact base, not a broken trend. What would settle it: a close above €184.3 (5-Aug) on 1.5x the 50D average or more restores the breakout; a close below €170.4 (28-Oct), beside the 50D MA, on above-average volume breaks the rising lows and starts the 10-day stop-loss test.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Basis: the ratios leave out the 18-Sep option-expiry and index-review day (50D average about 62,000 shares); the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's ratios, on its 50D average of 63,811 shares, are in brackets.

D'Ieteren's breakout was sold on the two sessions after it and bought back on the third on light volume.

  • 4-Nov (the breakout): +3.3% to €182.8 on 68,663 shares (€12.6m), 1.14x (1.11x).
  • 5-Nov: up to €187.0 intraday, closed at the low, €182.3, (0.3%), on 91,921 shares (€16.8m), 1.49x (1.46x), with Consumer discretionary up 0.1%: a distribution day.
  • 6-Nov: opened 1.0% lower, closed €178.6, (2.0%), on 73,798 shares (€13.2m), 1.18x (1.15x), with the Stoxx Europe 600 down 1.0%.
  • 9-Nov: €181.5, +1.6%, at 0.79 of the range, no gap, on 40,332 shares (€7.3m), 0.65x (0.63x), the lightest session since 30-Oct.
  • Inference: D'Ieteren fell 2.3% on 165,719 shares over 5 and 6-Nov and recovered 1.6% on a quarter of that: sellers met the top of the range, and buyers have not returned in size.

The heavy-volume days (1.5x the 50D average or more) of the last 25 sessions are still the four of 6 to 15-Oct: 6-Oct (+0.9% on 1.61x), 8-Oct (+2.2% on 2.72x, €22.9m, the Belron banks report), 9-Oct (+2.5% on 1.59x) and 15-Oct (+0.1% on 1.87x, €17.7m, closing at the low while its industry rose 1.6%). 5-Nov repeats 15-Oct; after 15-Oct D'Ieteren rose 2.0% the next session, then fell to €170.4 by 28-Oct. 9-Nov matches the first half of that pattern so far.

Distribution and accumulation: the volume script counts 3 distribution days (7-Oct, 14-Oct, 5-Nov) and 4 accumulation days (6, 8, 9 and 15-Oct) in the 25 sessions to 9-Nov: accumulation at the start of the advance, the newest distribution day at its top. The dashboard counts 1 (5 on 12-Oct), leaving out 7 and 14-Oct, which fit its own definition (a data question carried from the 3, 5 and 6-Nov notes).

Up against down volume is at its lowest since 6-Oct: the dashboard's 20-session ratio (average up-day over average down-day volume) reads 0.87, from 1.16 on 3-Nov; its 5-session ratio reads 0.66, which raw shares for 3 to 9-Nov do not reproduce (0.74; a data question). Over the advance from 24-Sep the script reads 1.06 (4 heavy up days, none down): like the June to August advance (1.02, given back by 13.2%), short of March to April's (1.61).

Volume at the MAs, and precedent: D'Ieteren lost its 50D and 150D MAs on 28-Oct on 0.51x, the lightest of the 25 sessions, and regained them on 0.56x and 0.65x: sellers absent at the MAs, present at the top. After the last stall in this zone (4-Sep, €180.4) D'Ieteren fell 2.2% the next session on only 0.66x, and 11.3% by 24-Sep; that decline ended with heavy up days eight and ten sessions after the low. This time the first down days came on more volume, and the first rebound on less.

Relative price is back above the October high but below the 5-Nov high and its relative 200D MA: 98.73 on 9-Nov, from 98.15 on 6-Nov and 99.46 on 5-Nov (above 98.11 on 22-Oct, below 101.10 on 5-Aug); 15.9% below its 52W high (2-Feb), from 18.5% at 30-Oct, and 1.1% below its relative 200D MA (99.79), below which it has stayed since 6-Aug. Relative price confirmed the SP's break of the September and October highs, not of August's.

Peers' volume on 9-Nov: every sector peer traded below its 50D average (Inter Cars 0.39x, Sixt 0.53x): a light market day across the sector.

Would confirm the move continues: a close above €184.3 (5-Aug) on 1.5x the 50D average or more (about 93,000 shares; 95,700 on the dashboard's basis). Would confirm a turn down: a close below €170.4 (28-Oct) on above-average volume, at or below the 50D MA.

3. RS

D'Ieteren's Stock RS read 50 on Mon 9-Nov, back to its 5-Nov high, with Sector RS (58) and Industry RS (53) unchanged since Friday: the groups carry it, and the stock has caught back up on a market day.

  • Stock RS: 50, from 44 at the Fri 6-Nov reading and 35 at the 30-Oct month-end; 47 a month ago (9-Oct), 40 three months ago, 22 twelve months ago. It stands exactly on the 50 line of the Sell Criterion "SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50", 20 below the 70 line.
  • Sector RS (Automotive - retail, aftermarket, leasing and rental): 58, from 29 on 29-Oct (its 12-month low was 27, on 7-Oct); 66 three months ago; 8 points above its 50 line.
  • Industry RS (Consumer discretionary): 53 at Fri 6-Nov, its highest in the 13 months stored, from 26 at every Friday from 9 to 30-Oct; 23 points above its 30 line.

The order of the turn: the sector first (from 29-Oct), the stock with it, the industry last (from 4-Nov). Stock RS has now made 50 twice (5 and 9-Nov) and has not broken above it. Inference: the rotation is still the driver; D'Ieteren's own leadership would show as Stock RS above 50 while Sector RS holds.

Precedent: D'Ieteren's autumn Stock RS rallies faded from about this level (56 on 4-Sep to 34 by 30-Sep; 47 on 9-Oct to 31 by 29-Oct), while from its two earlier lows Stock RS led the +37.0% and +16.0% advances (to 61 by 23-Jan-2026 and 56 by 1-Jul). A reading above 56 would be the autumn's first higher high.

The 6-Nov note's tests at Fri 13-Nov: would confirm the turn, Stock RS above 50 with Sector RS above 50 (today 50 and 58, one point short); would refute it, relative price below 98.11 with Stock RS below 47 (today 98.73 and 50). The 30-Nov month-end: Stock RS at 50 or more clears the Sell Criterion "SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50" whatever revenue Net Upgrade Breadth reads.

4. Consensus by named FY

D'Ieteren's consensus has not moved since 27-Oct: the SP rose 13.4% from 24-Sep on estimates cut in every FY over 3 and 6 months, and the price lead has not yet drawn an upgrade.

  • EPS, change over 1, 3 and 6 months: FY2026 (year to Dec-26) €12.75: (2.4%), (4.8%), (6.3%); FY2027 €15.09: (1.7%), (3.5%), (4.8%); FY2028 €17.38: (1.9%), (3.8%), (3.5%). Over 12 months the cuts are (13.8%), (12.5%) and (7.2%).
  • Revenue and EBITDA: unchanged since 30-Sep, after 3-month cuts of (1.5%) to (2.4%) in revenue and (3.3%) to (5.9%) in EBITDA following the 9-Sep half-year results.
  • The trajectory: the nearest year is cut fastest and the recovery pushed out: implied EPS growth is 18.4% to FY2027 and 15.2% to FY2028; the implied FY2027 EBITDA margin (EBITDA over revenue) fell to 8.3% from 8.5% three months ago.

The consolidated revenue and EBITDA lines measure mainly D'Ieteren Automotive, because Belron (50.3%) and TVH (40%) are equity-accounted (the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo). The revenue cuts and the (56%) revenue Net Upgrade Breadth track D'Ieteren Automotive, whose first-half profit before tax fell 66.6%, 51% below consensus (earnings review of the 9-Sep results); EPS carries Belron, which beat by 5%, and is cut less.

Your 3-Nov rules. On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". D'Ieteren still fails it in every FY. The same note says "Almost always, price leads estimates (when a sector is low in range)": D'Ieteren's rise from 24-Sep fits that lead, and the 5 and 6-Nov selling is what such a lead looks like when it stalls without estimate support. Against an upgrade soon: the A&J Memo expects FY2027 consensus to be cut 2% to 4% after a cautious framing at the 10-Mar-2027 results. Test: the 30-Nov month-end breadth for EPS and revenue.

  • For your ruling: the upgrades rule is not in the Position Entry and Exit Rules, so for an Early Innings Probing Bet it neither sells nor blocks an add; whether it applies to D'Ieteren is yours.

On your investor relations call. On 4-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "D'Ieteren is up from 165 to 175 L1M, and up 5% in Nov-26. My concern re. a weak IR call still remains". D'Ieteren is up 5.0% in November and 7.3% over the month; the 15-Oct call's content is still not filed. After it came a close at the low on 1.87x (15-Oct), an average PT step down (€239.5 to €237.3 by 23-Oct), a 1.7% FY2027 EPS cut (27-Oct) and the two heavy down days at the top of the range. Hypothesis: holders who share your concern sell into strength; no source read says so.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

D'Ieteren's analysts rate it Buy unanimously while every estimate Net Upgrade Breadth is negative: the ratings and PTs value Belron, the estimates track D'Ieteren Automotive. Nothing changed since 6-Nov.

  • Ratings: 14 Buys of 14, from 6 of 7 twenty-four months ago; every analyst has rated D'Ieteren Buy at every month-end since January 2026, through the 21.3% and 13.2% falls: the ratings follow neither valuation nor momentum.
  • Average PT: €234.7, (2.0%) since 8-Oct (€239.5), +10.5% over 12 months (€212.4 at 31-Oct-2025). The average PT's gap above the SP narrowed to 29.3% from 31.4% on 6-Nov, because the SP rose; in 2026 it has run from 12.6% (10-Feb high) to 51.4% (30-Mar low) with the PT between €220 and €240, so the gap moves with the SP alone. The latest named changes were raises on a higher Belron value: KBC Securities to €250 from €245 (Bolero, 16-Sep) and ING to €263 from €237 (Zone Bourse, 28-Sep).

Net Upgrade Breadth, unchanged since 2-Nov: FY2027 EPS (21%) (1 raising, 4 cutting, of 14), from +9% at 31-Aug; EBITDA (62%) (0 and 5, of 8); revenue (56%) (0 and 5, of 9); PT +29% (5 and 1, of 14), easing from +46% at 30-Sep and +36% at 30-Oct. No analyst has raised revenue since the March month-end or EBITDA since May: the PTs carry a Belron listing value, the estimates D'Ieteren Automotive's decline.

Where analysts are cutting: D'Ieteren Automotive. Deutsche Bank's Kerstens cut profit before tax forecasts by 1% after the 9-Sep results "to reflect the heavier-than-expected drag from Automotive retail operations" (earnings review). Jefferies expects Belgian fleet demand to "taper" from 2027 as full tax deductibility of corporate electric cars ends on 1-Jan-2027 (Jefferies, in the AlphaSense research filed on D'Ieteren). The A&J Memo records the widest dispute there: Bernstein's 1.7% FY2028 Automotive margin against Deutsche Bank's near 4%. The four analysts cutting FY2027 EPS and the five cutting revenue since September are not named in the sources read, and the one PT cut since 30-Sep (between 8 and 23-Oct) is not named either.

Precedent: the same split ran across the 20.9% rally to 17-Apr (PT breadth +30% and +18%, revenue breadth (25%) and (60%) at the March and April month-ends), most of which D'Ieteren gave back by 8-Jun. Test: PT cuts without Belron news would mean the Belron value is being cut; an EPS raise would be the first estimate support for the SP since August.

9. Peers

D'Ieteren's cohort holds no other stock, so its peers are its sector's (the page's peer table).

Since 24-Sep D'Ieteren has traded with the parts and rental half of its sector; this week that half paused and the broken car-retail and leasing half bounced, while D'Ieteren sat between them.

  • SP technicals: over the week to 9-Nov D'Ieteren rose 2.2%, behind Ayvens (+7.0%) and Bilia (+4.8%), level with Inter Cars (+1.5%), ahead of Sixt ((1.8%)) and Auto1 ((7.5%)). It reversed on 5 and 6-Nov with Inter Cars ((4.2%) on 5-Nov after +4.8%) and Sixt ((1.2%), (1.0%)). Ayvens has risen on five of six sessions, but at Stock RS 22 and 4.7% below its absolute 200D MA it is a bounce in a broken name, not yet a turn.
  • Sell-side estimates: D'Ieteren's revenue Net Upgrade Breadth ((56%)) and 3-month EPS revision ((4%)) sit with the broken half (Auto1 (64%) and (6%), Inchcape 3-month EPS (9%), Ayvens (33%)), against positive revenue Net Upgrade Breadth at ZIGUP (+33%), Sixt (+25%), Inter Cars (+20%) and Bilia (+14%); Inter Cars' 3-month EPS revision is +10%, Sixt's +2%. D'Ieteren's SP trades with the leaders and its estimates with the laggards.
  • RS: Inter Cars (96), ZIGUP (67), Bilia (62) and Sixt (59, from 46 a month ago) hold D'Ieteren's Sector RS of 58 up; Ayvens (22), Auto1 (7) and Inchcape (6) stay at the bottom.
  • Newsflow, guidance and communications: nothing for 9-Nov is in the sources read. The 6-Nov report carried one peer estimate change, upward: Jefferies raised Ayvens' FY2026 EPS 6.6%, keeping Hold and a €9.70 PT (Jefferies, 30-Oct, via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026). The latest guidance change on file is new-car retail's: Inchcape cut its FY2026 adjusted operating margin guidance to about 5.5% from about 6% (Hargreaves Lansdown, 22-Oct), D'Ieteren Automotive's business.

Who turned first: Sixt's Stock RS in October, then D'Ieteren's from 29-Oct; Inter Cars led the reversal on 5-Nov. Sixt tracks D'Ieteren's daily moves most closely (average correlation 0.28, weak); the A&J Memo finds the sector explains only 19% of D'Ieteren's daily variance, so peers set its tone, not its direction.

What the peers imply: Sixt's third-quarter results on Thu 12-Nov are the dated test: a beat that extends the rental turn supports D'Ieteren's Sector RS above 50 into the 30-Nov month-end; a miss would show whether 5 and 6-Nov began the leaders' profit-taking. A day on which D'Ieteren falls with Ayvens, Inchcape and Auto1 without Belron news would be the first sign that its car arm sets the price.

What the rules say for D'Ieteren now, and what decides the next step

The three findings that matter most:

  • The breakout stalled and the rebound is the market's: the 4-Nov close above €180.4 reversed on 1.49x and 1.18x; 9-Nov's 1.6% rise came on 0.65x, the market explaining about 1.5 points. The lows still rise and the SP is above every MA: the base is intact.
  • The groups carry the stock: Industry RS 53 (a 13-month high) and Sector RS 58 stand well above their lines; Stock RS is back to 50, not above it.
  • Estimates have not followed the price: EPS is cut in every FY over 3 and 6 months and unchanged since 27-Oct, so D'Ieteren still fails your 3-Nov upgrades rule; the Buys and PTs carry a Belron value the estimates do not.

Tranche 1 (2.61% now, 2.5% at entry, bought 12-Aug, on the 50D MA): clear. 50D MA €170.77, stop-loss €160.52, close €181.5: a 5.9% fall reaches the MA, 11.6% the stop-loss. Tranche 1's exit confirmed on 3-Sep-2026 was cleared by your 2-Nov ruling (the 10-day stop-loss rule applies from 2-Nov; a confirmation before it whose price had closed back above the MA was cleared); judged afresh from 2-Nov, D'Ieteren has not closed at or below the 50D MA.

The nearest sell tests:

  • The 10-day stop-loss rule is nearest, and moved away on 9-Nov: the close stood 6.3% above the 50D MA on 9-Nov, 4.6% on 6-Nov, 4.2% a week earlier, 1.4% below four weeks earlier (12-Oct) and 1.9% above thirteen weeks earlier (12-Aug). A 5.9% fall equals 1.6 of D'Ieteren's usual weekly moves (3.6% each); at a flat SP the rising MA (€173.68 by 30-Nov) narrows it to 4.3%.
  • How a test would end depends on its volume: from day 2 the exit is confirmed when the volume after day 1 averages 0.75x the 50D average. D'Ieteren's August and 28-Oct tests (0.51x to 0.74x) ended unconfirmed; its 2 to 3-Sep test confirmed on 1.10x. A pullback to the MA on last week's volume (0.88x to 1.46x, dashboard) would likely confirm on day 2 and sell at the following Tuesday session; one on 9-Nov's 0.63x would not.
  • €160 to €162, 11% to 12% below the close, is where three lines meet: the stop-loss (€160.52), relative price 25% below its 52W high (€161.88 at a flat market) and the 24-Sep low; the MA test acts long before.
  • The six Sell Criteria, information only: at Fri 13-Nov the relative 200D MA will read as falling whatever D'Ieteren does ((0.90%) today, about (1.29%) at a flat relative price), as February's relative prices (114 to 117, against 98.7) leave its window. Industry RS (53) is 23 points above its 30 line. At 30-Nov Stock RS decides the breadth test: it reads 50, exactly on the line, with revenue Net Upgrade Breadth at (56%); Sector RS (58) clears its test if it holds above 50. A fall to the 50D MA would likely take Stock RS back below 50 and so put the Net Upgrade Breadth test back to giving a sell signal for a Core Investment; the Sector RS test would also need Sector RS (58) back below 50, which a D'Ieteren fall alone does not do; for this Probing Bet only the MA test sells.

The record: under today's rule tranche 1's first exit would have been confirmed on 3-Sep, selling at the Tue 8-Sep session at about €174.1. D'Ieteren then fell 8.1% to €160.0 and now stands 4.3% above that price: the rule would have avoided the fall and missed the recovery, at a cost of about 4%.

What decides the next step:

  • The rules allow an add: tranche 1 is clear; 3.39% of room below the 6% limit on current value; the next tranche would be 3.0%. Consumer discretionary holdings total 4.48% of capital (with Ballys Intralot), far inside the 30% cap.
  • Where D'Ieteren stands for an add: the first stage, "pulling back or basing", which this note judges to be basing; the Master Dashboard reads none of the three (item 12 (adding capital)): basing, because the SP is back inside the €160 to €187 range with rising lows and the 4-Nov breakout reversed. No add trigger is fully present; your 3-Nov "up-sizing of probing bets when they break out" finds no breakout at the 9-Nov close.
  • Dates: Sixt's third-quarter results, Thu 12-Nov; the next reading day, Fri 13-Nov (Industry RS; the relative 200D MA); the MA order turns on 24-Nov at a flat SP; the 30-Nov month-end; a new chief executive, Eric Machiels, from December; FY2026 results, 10-Mar-2027; Belron listing news at any time.
  • What would change this reading: a close above €184.3 on 1.5x the 50D average or more (the third stage, breaking out); a close at or below the 50D MA (€170.77), which starts the 10-day test and blocks an add while it runs.

Richard decides.

What the rules say for Fevertree now, and what decides the next step

The findings that matter most:

  • Fevertree is stuck under a ceiling at its flat 947.3p average PT: six intraday pushes to 943p to 955p since 23-Oct, no close above 943.5p, and 9-Nov's 2.2% gap up sold back by the close for a cause not in the sources read; under it, intraday lows keep rising and volume is light (items 1, 2, 5 and 6).
  • Fevertree is losing ground inside a rotation that is still running: (1.8%) over the week against Consumer staples' +1.5%; Stock RS 58, from 68 on 23-Oct, while Beverages' Sector RS holds at 78; this week it traded with the small soft-drinks names, not the alcohol makers (items 1, 3 and 9).
  • Nothing fundamental is pulling the other way: no FY2026 to FY2028 estimate up over 3 months, the later years cut fastest, revenue and PT Net Upgrade Breadth negative, and no United States scanner print since the 4-Nov tracker's 11.9% to 15.4% (items 4, 5 and 6).

Tranche 1 (2.47% of capital now, 2.0% at entry; 50D MA) is clear under the 10-day stop-loss rule: the close of 926.5p is 6.7% above the 50D MA (868.2p) and 13.5% above the stop-loss (816.1p, the MA less 6%). Under your 2-Nov-2026 ruling the rule applies from that day and confirmations before it whose price had closed back above the MA were cleared; tranche 1's exit confirmed on 3-Sep-2026 was cleared, and the tranche is judged afresh from Monday 2-Nov.

The nearest sell test is tranche 1's 50D MA, and the rising 50D is closing the gap.

  • Pace: a 6.3% fall from the close starts a test; the close stood 9.6% above the 50D on 2-Nov (1 week), 2.0% on 9-Oct (4 weeks) and 5.5% on 10-Aug (13 weeks). At a flat SP the 50D reaches 876.3p by Friday 13-Nov and 889.3p by Friday 20-Nov, cutting the fall needed to 5.4% and then 4.0%; the stop-loss rises to 823.8p and 835.9p.
  • What the rule then does: Fevertree's down days since 22-Oct ran 0.76x to 1.32x the 50D average, so a second close at or below the 50D on ordinary volume would very likely confirm the exit on day 2 (the days-after volume average reaching 0.75 times the 50-day average), selling tranche 1 at the following Tuesday session (17-Nov at the earliest). A fall to the stop-loss would cost about 0.29% of capital.
  • How the tests interact: the same fall would very likely take Stock RS below 50 (it lost 11 points on a 2.9% fall from 23 to 29-Oct), which with negative revenue and PT Net Upgrade Breadth would give a Core Investment a sell signal at the 30-Nov month-end; for Fevertree it is information only.
